# Wayfinder Corsair Campaign Setting & Rulebook

# Chapter 1: Campaign Framework

## The World: The Shattered Expanse
Nearly 800 years ago came **The Sundering Tide**. No one knows exactly what happened—whether the gods went to war, an elder civilization awakened something beneath the crust, or the oceans became sentient. The world shattered into tens of thousands of islands. New islands emerge, entire nations drift, and maps are never permanent.

Only about 35% of the world's oceans have reliable charts. The rest is **The Unwritten Sea**.

## Core Campaign Blend
This campaign is an exploration sandbox blending "Age of Sail" themes with "High Fantasy" elements:
- **The Uncharted Ocean (40%)**: The world is not fully known. Maps are wrong. The primary gameplay loop is Rumor → Expedition → Discovery → Map → Route → Trade → Conflict.
- **The Great Magical Trade Network (35%)**: The economic engine. Discoveries matter because new resources (e.g., Moon Coral) create trade opportunities, drawing merchants, pirates, and governments.
- **The Pirate Renaissance (20%)**: Pirates are the first people willing to go where governments cannot. The era creates factions—traditional raiders, pirate nobility, independent explorers.
- **The Underdark Ocean (15%)**: A massive subterranean ecosystem containing ancient cities, drowned kingdoms, and alien ecosystems like the Aboleth Archives and Drow courts.
- **The Lost Civilization Mystery (100% Underlying Engine)**: Why are oceans unexplored? Because someone made them unexplorable. Everything connects back to **The First Captains**.

## The First Captains
The original explorers. The first people to bond with ships and spirits. They mastered the connection between Arcane, Psionics, Spirits, and the Ocean. At some point in history, they vanished. 

> [!NOTE] 
> **Level 20 Capstone Quest:** Upon reaching level 20, a Wayfinder is offered a legendary quest to "Restore the First Captains." This world-altering choice asks the player to restore, replace, hide, or release the greatest explorers in history.

## The Four Magic Systems
Magic enhances naval warfare rather than replacing it. Ships remain the kings of warfare.
1. **Arcane (The Pact)**: Traditional magic and warlock pacts. Power borrowed from sea devils, leviathans, living storms, or star beings. "I borrowed power from something older than the world."
2. **Psionics (The Mind)**: Power originating from consciousness itself. Some surface kingdoms fear it; the greatest psionic cities lie beneath the earth. "I discovered that my own consciousness is a weapon."
3. **Spirit (The Tide)**: Primal sea magic. Not worship, but alignment with the ocean's natural order. "I learned the hidden laws of the sea itself."
4. **Deep Magic**: Predates all three. It changes gravity, distance, probability, and time. It is the source of many impossible places and artifacts.

## Major Factions
The ocean is a deeply political ecosystem:
- **The Free Captains Alliance (Blackwake Harbor)**: A pirate frontier valuing exploration and independence.
- **The Grand Merchant Consortium (Port Meridian)**: A neutral trade power that doesn't own the city, just everything inside it.
- **The Crimson Dominion (The Crimson Citadel)**: A wealthy arcane empire built on slavery, magical superiority, and the exploitation of conquered peoples. They view Wayfinders and new routes as economic threats.
- **The Drowned Silk Houses (Nymorath Deep Harbor)**: An ancient Drow Underdark Ocean civilization. They raid the surface for resources, ancient claims, and political warfare. They remember the First Captains.
- **The Spirit Keepers (The Last Harbor)**: A hidden sanctuary of guardians preserving old traditions, Navigator Spirits, and First Captain lore.
- **Independent Salvagers (Salvager's Haven)**: A frontier port of wreck hunters, explorers, and forgotten discoveries.


---

# Chapter 2: Starting Origins

Every Wayfinder campaign begins with a shipwreck. The party does not start with a ship; they start with survival. 

## The Tide's Choice
There are two opening methods to determine where and how the players begin their adventure.

### Method A: The Captain's Choice
*“I chose where I wanted to go. The sea decided how I arrived.”*

The player chooses their **Home Port** and starting faction environment. 
The sea (via random dice rolls or GM selection) determines:
- Shipwreck Cause
- Rescue Vessel
- Rescue Circumstances
- Initial Reputation

### Method B: The Sea's Choice
*“I surrendered my destination, but I wrote my legend.”*

The player rolls randomly for their **Home Port**, letting fate decide their starting faction, local allies, and initial enemies. 
In return, the player chooses:
- Shipwreck Cause
- Rescue Vessel
- Arrival Circumstance

## Shipwreck Causes
The reason the ship went down shapes the player's initial mystery and backstory. Causes include:
- Leviathan attack
- Magical storm / Psionic distortion
- Drow naval assault / Crimson Dominion experiment
- Pirate battle / Mutiny / Betrayal
- Ancient defense system activated
- Underdark Ocean current breach / Planar ocean anomaly
- Ship vanished and reappeared damaged

## Rescue Vessels & Circumstances
The rescuers do not necessarily know they are rescuing someone important. The ship that pulls you from the water determines your first relationship and faction tie.

### Rescuer Options
- **Blackwake Connection:** A pirate exploration vessel hunting for treasure.
- **Port Meridian Connection:** A merchant expedition charting routes.
- **Crimson Dominion Connection:** A slaver/arcane vessel that recognizes something unusual about your survival.
- **Drow Connection:** Drow scouts drifting near an Underdark Ocean route.
- **Spirit Keeper Connection:** A vessel guided by spirits, deliberately looking for you.
- **Salvager Connection:** Wreck hunters where you are just another survivor.

### Arrival Circumstances
How you are treated on the rescue ship heavily influences your arrival at the Home Port:
- **Honored Guest:** Treated well.
- **Prisoner:** Rescued, but questioned and held under suspicion.
- **Debt Owed:** Saved, but expecting repayment.
- **Mystery Passenger:** The crew does not understand why you survived.
- **Prophecy/Recognition:** Someone on the ship knows exactly what you are.


---

# Chapter 3: Races & Transformations

While the campaign setting supports many races across the Shattered Expanse, the example base race for this specific Wayfinder is the **Tideborn Pixie**. However, characters do not stay "just" their starting race. As they explore the oceans, they undergo the **Fey Transformation System**, accumulating templates that reflect the magic, ruins, and pacts they encounter. 

## Base Race: Tideborn Pixie
"A Pixie born from ocean currents, ancient routes, and the forgotten magic between shores."

**Type:** Fey (Small)
**Speed:** 20 ft. Land / 60 ft. Fly (Good) / 30 ft. Swim (Good)
**Ability Adjustments:** Str -4, Dex +8, Int +6, Wis +4, Cha +8

**Racial Traits:**
- **Tideborn Amphibious Nature:** Ignores underwater movement penalties and natural ocean terrain penalties. Can breathe underwater 1 hr/HD/day (permanent at 5 HD).
- **Fey Sight:** Low-light vision. **Spirit Sight:** Detect Fey/Spirits/Magical Residue at will (30 ft. range).
- **Fey Magic:** Original 3.0 Pixie Spell-Like Abilities.
- **Polymorph:** The original 3.0 Pixie Polymorph ability (self only). Cannot gain spell-like, supernatural, or extraordinary abilities from the new form.
- **Psionic Spark:** Base Power Points = 1/2 Character Level + Cha Mod (min 1). Start with one power: *Detect Psionics, Far Hand*, or *Minor Metamorphosis*.

## The Fey Transformation System
Players may acquire transformations during their journeys. These are not simply chosen from a menu; they are earned or inflicted by the world.

**Transformation Capacity:** A character has a maximum of **4 Transformation Points**.
**Ability Score Stacking Rule:** No ability score can gain more than a +6 total bonus strictly from templates. (Base Tideborn Pixie bonuses are unaffected).

### Category A: Minor Marks
**Limit:** Max 2 | **Cost:** 1 Transformation Point
Minor changes caused by magical exposure, blessings, mutations, or small awakenings.
- **Fey-Touched (+2 Cha):** Pure fey awakening. +2 Diplomacy/Bluff/Perform. *Minor Glamour* (Silent Image 1/day). 
- **Stormborn Fey (+2 Dex):** Ocean storms. Electricity Resist 5. Wind Rider (flight maneuverability up one step). Storm Sense (+2 Spot, Survival, Sailor).
- **Coral-Blooded Fey (+2 Con):** Reef adaptation. Natural Armor +1. Improved underwater survival.
- **Moonlit Fey (+2 Wis):** Lunar tide magic. Darkvision 30 ft. Lunar Grace (+2 Dodge AC at night). Dream Whisper 1/day.
- **Shadowwake Fey (+2 Dex):** Dark ocean. 10% concealment in dim light. Darkvision 30 ft. Shadow Step (10 ft. teleport between shadows 1/day).
- **Psionic Spark Fey (+2 Int):** Mind awakening. +3 PP. Minor Psionic Talent (one power). +1 vs mind-affecting.
- **Spirit Marked Fey (+2 Wis):** Spirit connection. Gain a minor spirit companion. Spirit Awareness (30 ft.). +1 resistance to saves.
- **Draconic Spark Fey (+2 Con):** Dormant dragon ancestry. Resist 5 to one element. Blindsense 30 ft. Minor breath weapon (1/day, 1d4, 15ft cone).
- **Arcane Marked Fey (+2 Int):** Minor magical mutation. +1 against spells. Minor arcane trick (cantrip at will). +1 caster level for one school.

### Category B: Awakening Templates
**Limit:** Max 1 | **Cost:** 2 Transformation Points
A fundamental evolution of the character.
- **Awakened Fey (+2 Int, +2 Wis, +2 Cha):** Psionic evolution. Gains Psionic subtype. PP = HD + Int + Cha. One Psionic Discipline & 1st level power. +4 vs enchantments/mind-affecting. Lifespan x5, no aging penalties.
- **Arcane Ascendant Fey (+2 Int, +2 Cha):** Deep magic conduit. Gains Arcane Reservoir (1 bonus spell slot lower than max). Spellweaver Soul (+2 caster checks for one school). Resistance 10 to one element. Arcane Memory (recall witnessed spell 1/day).
- **Spirit-Bound Fey (+2 Wis, +2 Cha):** Spirit Shaman evolution. Navigator Spirit manifests physically. +2 on spirit-related checks. Ancestral Memory (+10 Knowledge 1/day). Spirit Walk (ethereal 1 round/level, 1/day).
- **First Captain Legacy Fey (+2 Int, +2 Wis, +2 Cha):** Lost Wayfinder inheritance. +4 Sailor, Survival, Geography. Captain's Echo (visions from past Wayfinders). Improved Ship Bond progression. Lost Route Memory (recognize ancient routes 1/week).

### Category C: Heritage Templates
**Limit:** Max 1 | **Cost:** 3 Transformation Points
Changes what the character is fundamentally.
- **Draconic Fey (+2 Str, +2 Con, +2 Int, +2 Cha):** Dragon lineage. Element Resistance 10. Blindsense 30 ft. Breath weapon (1d6/HD, max 10d6, 1/day).
- **Celestial Tide Fey (+2 Con, +2 Wis, +2 Cha):** Divine blessing. Good alignment. Acid/Cold/Elec Resist 10. Divine Grace (Cha to saves). Radiant Presence (+1 morale to allies' saves).
- **Infernal Fey (+2 Con, +2 Int, +4 Cha):** Infernal pact. Lawful alignment. Fire Resist 15. Contract Mind (+4 vs charm/domination). Infernal Boon (Hellfire or Binding Word).
- **Xoriat-Touched Fey (+4 Int, +2 Wis, -2 Cha):** Reality mutation. Aberration subtype. Immune to confusion/insanity. Reality Fracture (reroll 1 failed d20 per day). Impossible Anatomy (20% miss chance).
- **Chimera Fey (+2 Str, +4 Con, +2 Int):** Engineered evolution. Gain two physical mutations (Predator, Aquatic, Winged, Armored, etc.). Mutable Anatomy (swap one mutation 1/day). +4 vs polymorph/transmutation.

### Category D: Mythic Transformations (Campaign Locked)
Not purchased. Not selected. Earned through world-altering campaign events.
- **Deep-Touched Fey (+4 Con, +2 Int, +2 Wis, +2 Cha):** Altered by the Underdark Ocean. Aberration/Aquatic traits. Darkvision 120 ft., Water breathing, Pressure immunity, Cold Resist 10. Abyssal Telepathy 60 ft. Deep Resonance (Bonus PP = Level + Cha). Unlocks an entire mythic evolution tree at levels 5, 10, 15, and 20.
- **Abyssal Fey (+2 Con, +2 Int, +4 Cha):** Corrupted ocean magic. Evil alignment. Dark Tide abilities. Abyssal Mark (gain power but attract outsider attention). Speak with dead sailors.

# Chapter 4: The Wayfinder Class

"A Wayfinder is not a swashbuckler with magic, a warlock with a boat, or a psion with a sword. The Wayfinder is the rediscovery of an ancient First Captain tradition where Arcane magic shapes reality, Psionics shapes perception, and Spirit magic shapes connection. At low levels, these are three separate disciplines. At high levels, the Wayfinder becomes someone who does not simply navigate the world—they alter the routes the world allows."

## Class Chassis
**Hit Die:** d8
**Alignment:** Any
**Class Skills:** 8 + Int modifier per level. (Balance, Bluff, Concentration, Craft (Ships), Decipher Script, Diplomacy, Gather Information, Handle Animal, Intimidate, Knowledge (Arcana/Geography/History/Local/Nature/Psionics/Religion), Profession (Sailor), Search, Sense Motive, Spellcraft, Spot, Survival, Swim, UMD, UPD).
**Base Attack Bonus:** Medium (+15 at Level 20).
**Saving Throws:** Good Reflex and Will, Poor Fortitude.
**Weapon & Armor Proficiency:** Simple weapons, martial light weapons, rapier, cutlass, hand crossbow, shortbow, longbow, firearms. Light armor (Medium at level 5), shields (except tower).

## The Threefold Magic Systems
The Wayfinder progresses in three magical paths simultaneously.
1. **The Pact (Arcane):** Warlock chassis. Uses Charisma. Eldritch Armament (can manifest Eldritch Blast through a weapon, harpoon, pistol, or standard blast) and Invocations.
2. **The Mind (Psionic):** Psion chassis (half-progression, max 6th-level powers). Uses Charisma. Gains Power Points and discovers powers.
3. **The Tide (Spirit):** Custom Spirit Shaman+ chassis. Full 1-9 spell progression but spells must be discovered/learned via the Captain's Log instead of prepared from a deity.

### Lore-Caster (The Fusion Engine)
Instead of preparing spells, the Wayfinder uncovers **Wayfinding Lore**—an accumulated understanding of the world. 
- **Level 7 (Arcane-Psionic Fusion):** Combine a spell and a power into one action (e.g., Fireball + Telekinesis = Moving Flame Sphere).
- **Level 10 (Threefold Awakening - Triune Casting):** 1/day, combine a Spell, a Power, and a Spirit Ability to open new paths to unseen locations.
- **Level 17 (Reality Navigation):** You do not teleport; you discover a path through solid fog, magical barriers, or impassable storms.
- **Level 20 (Creator of Paths):** Create permanent routes, impossible trade lanes, and open new ocean passages.

## The Navigator Spirit
At level 1, the Wayfinder awakens a personal spirit companion—the "mirror" of the captain.
1. **Origin Seed:** Choose the spirit's foundational nature (e.g., Ancient Navigator, Stormborn, Abyssal, Leviathan, Ancestor Captain, Ghost Fleet).
2. **Seed Benefit:** A deliberate expression of the spirit (e.g., Storm Sense, Lost Routes, Deep Survival).
3. **Random Traits:** Roll 2d100 for an Innate Trait and an Emergent Trait (e.g., Ancient Memory, Hates Calm Waters, Echo of Darkness).
4. **Evolution:** The spirit evolves at levels 5, 10, 15, and 20 based on the captain's actions (e.g., Guardian Aspect, Sage Aspect, Dominator Aspect, Pathfinder Aspect). At level 20, it becomes the embodiment of your legend.

## The Living Ship Bond
The ship is your second character. It advances through **Vessel Tiers** via Wayfinder level, Chronicle Achievements, and Physical Transformation.
- **Tier 0 (Mortal Ship):** Normal vessel with maintenance costs.
- **Tier 1 (Claimed Vessel - Lvl 3):** Ship recognizes you. You sense its condition.
- **Tier 2 (Awakened Vessel - Lvl 6):** Gains intelligence/personality. Becomes an Arcane, Psionic, or Tide vessel.
- **Tier 3 (Legendary Vessel - Lvl 10):** Threefold Keel. Can install Arcane, Psionic, and Tide upgrades (e.g., Eldritch Cannons, Psychic Helm, Storm Calling).
- **Tier 4 (Mythic Vessel - Lvl 15):** Requires rare materials (dragon bone, deep crystal). Gains traits like Ghost Ship or World Sailor.
- **Tier 5 (Path-Maker Vessel - Lvl 20):** Creates permanent passages through reality. The ship changes the map of the world.

## Captain's Techniques
In addition to their magical and vessel progression, a Wayfinder gains **Captain's Techniques** at levels 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, and 18. These represent their growing influence over the sea, their crew, and the world itself. 

*See **Chapter 5a: Captain's Techniques (Rules & Framework)** for acquisition rules and **Chapter 11: Progression Charts** for category and tier advancement summaries.*


---

# Chapter 5a: Captain's Techniques (Rules & Framework)

*“Every captain leaves behind stories. The First Captains left behind techniques.”*

A Captain's Technique is not a spell, a maneuver, or a psionic power. It is a legendary trick, habit, doctrine, or philosophy that has become supernatural through repetition and authority. The Wayfinder learns 6 Captain's Techniques over their career (at levels 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18), though class features/milestones may grant more.

## Mechanics
- **Save DC Formula:** `10 + 1/2 Wayfinder level + Charisma Modifier`.
- **Usage Limits:** Unless stated otherwise, they are generally at-will or have specific per-encounter/per-day limits outlined in their text.
- **Action Types:** Standard D&D 3.5e actions (Standard, Move, Swift, Immediate, Passive/Special).
  - *Global Rule:* A Wayfinder may only use **one Immediate Action Captain's Technique per round** to prevent reaction bloat.

## Categories
Each category has a unique gameplay identity. If a technique solves a problem, the category defines *how* it solves it.
1. **Seamanship:** Environmental mastery. Navigation, ship handling, weather, avoiding hazards. "How do I move through the world?"
2. **Duelist:** Personal combat mastery. Precision, ripostes, officer challenges, exploiting openings. "How do I win this fight?"
3. **Corsair:** Aggressive raider. Boarding, fear, plunder, breaking defenses (siege). "How do I break the enemy?" *(Note: Corsair siege techniques create vulnerabilities, not automatic destruction).*
4. **Fleet:** Tactical commander. Coordination, formations, positioning. "How do we fight as one?" *(Note: A creature can benefit from only one Fleet command effect at a time).*
5. **Hunter:** Tracking and pursuit. Reconnaissance, interception, elimination. "How do I find what is hidden?"
6. **Mystic:** Keeper of sea secrets. Sea magic, omens, supernatural navigation. "What does the world reveal?" *(Note: Mystic info provides clues/probabilities, not perfect answers).*
7. **First Captain:** Sovereign commander. Leadership, territory, diplomacy, political authority. "How do I build influence?" *(Note: Creates leverage and reputation, not automatic obedience).*

## The Tiers
1. **Least (Levels 1-5):** *The Deckhand Becomes an Officer.* Focuses on personal skill, small tactical advantages, basic navigation. Scale: Self/1 Ally.
2. **Lesser (Levels 6-11):** *The Officer Becomes a Captain.* Focuses on crew coordination, resource efficiency, and boarding dominance. Scale: Small squad/Ship.
3. **Greater (Levels 12-17):** *The Captain Becomes an Admiral.* Encounter-defining commands, fleet tactics, environmental manipulation. Scale: Entire battlefield.
4. **Legendary (Levels 18-20):** *The Admiral Becomes the First Captain.* Maritime sovereignty, campaign-scale influence, world map changes. Scale: Regions/Factions/Campaigns. *(Note: Permanent world-changing effects require time, resources, and narrative consequences).*


---

# Chapter 5b: Least Captain's Techniques (Levels 1-5)

These techniques establish the Wayfinder's earliest identity. They emphasize positioning, environment, precision, and skill over raw statistical bonuses.
## Seamanship Techniques

### Sea Legs
**Technique Name:** Sea Legs  
**Category:** Seamanship  
**Tier:** Least  
**Level Band:** Levels 1-5  
**Type:** Extraordinary  
**Action Type:** Passive  
**Range/Target:** Self  
**Duration:** Instantaneous  
**Saving Throw:** None  
**Prerequisites:** None  

**Effect:**  
Ignore movement penalties from pitching decks, slick surfaces, rigging. Stand from prone as a swift action on a vessel.  

**Scaling:**  
None  

**Flavor Text:**  
*A display of the Wayfinder's growing mastery.*

### Chart the Unknown
**Technique Name:** Chart the Unknown  
**Category:** Seamanship  
**Tier:** Least  
**Level Band:** Levels 1-5  
**Type:** Extraordinary  
**Action Type:** Standard  
**Range/Target:** Area within 1 mile  
**Duration:** Instantaneous  
**Saving Throw:** None  
**Prerequisites:** None  

**Effect:**  
Study terrain/currents for 1 min. Create a mental chart. Learn safe routes, unexplored regions, or if a route is artificial. (Reveals geography only).  

**Scaling:**  
None  

**Flavor Text:**  
*A display of the Wayfinder's growing mastery.*

**Technique Name:** Favor the Current

**Type:** Extraordinary  
**Action Type:** Swift  
**Range/Target:** Self  
**Duration:** 1 round  
**Saving Throw:** None  
**Prerequisites:** None  

**Effect:**  
Ignore difficult terrain caused by water, mud, sand, snow, or loose stone for 1 round.  

**Scaling:**  
None  

**Flavor Text:**  
*A display of the Wayfinder's growing mastery.*

**Technique Name:** Mooring Line

# Chapter 5c: Lesser Captain's Techniques (Levels 6-11)

These techniques emphasize escort control, boarding pressure, command tempo, shipboard dominance, and field adaptability.

**Range/Target:** Self & Allies within 20 ft  
**Duration:** Instantaneous  
**Saving Throw:** None  
**Prerequisites:** None

# Chapter 5d: Greater Captain's Techniques (Levels 12-17)

These techniques shape encounters through movement control, battlefield pressure, crew coordination, tactical denial, and decisive command. They reshape terrain, tempo, and morale.
## Seamanship Techniques

**Range/Target:** Self & Allies 30 ft  
**Duration:** 1 round  
**Saving Throw:** None  
**Prerequisites:** None

# Chapter 5e: Legendary Captain's Techniques (Levels 18-20)

Legendary techniques alter voyages, fleets, exploration, and faction play. They are capstone-tier commands meant to change the map itself. *Note: Permanent world-changing effects require time, resources, and narrative consequences. They provide influence, opportunities, and strategic advantage, not automatic control.*
## Seamanship Techniques

### Crown the Course
**Technique Name:** Crown the Course  
**Category:** Seamanship  
**Tier:** Legendary  
**Level Band:** Levels 18-20  
**Type:** Extraordinary  
**Action Type:** 1-hour ritual  
**Range/Target:** Personal  
**Duration:** Instantaneous  
**Saving Throw:** None  
**Prerequisites:** None  

**Effect:**  
Name known destination on same plane. For 24 hrs, ship cannot become lost, ignores one nonmagical travel hazard, travels double pace if path exists.  

**Scaling:**  
None  

**Flavor Text:**  
*A display of the Wayfinder's growing mastery.*

### Sovereign Wake
**Technique Name:** Sovereign Wake  
**Category:** Seamanship  
**Tier:** Legendary  
**Level Band:** Levels 18-20  
**Type:** Extraordinary  
**Action Type:** Swift  
**Range/Target:** Allies 60 ft  
**Duration:** 1 min/level  
**Saving Throw:** None  
**Prerequisites:** None  

**Effect:**  
Allies ignore penalties from wind, waves, shallow water, motion. Move across water/unstable surfaces as firm ground.  

**Scaling:**  
None  

**Flavor Text:**  
*A display of the Wayfinder's growing mastery.*

None  

**Flavor Text:**

# Chapter 6: Campaign Engines

The campaign loop is: **Explore → Discover → Record → Understand → Upgrade → Explore Further**. Combat feeds discovery, and discovery changes the world.

## The Discovery System & Captain's Log
A Discovery is a verified, meaningful first-time revelation that expands the world. A rumor is not a discovery; it must be physically mapped, witnessed, or magically confirmed.
- **Wayfinding Lore:** Accumulated understanding. Never spent, only gained. Used to unlock Fusion Techniques, ship awakenings, and legendary status.
- **Discovery Points (DP):** Short-term resource spent on Fusion Techniques and special class abilities (max 3-10, refreshes during downtime).
- **The Captain's Log:** A magical, permanent record. It tracks discoveries across categories (Charts, Species, Trade, Magic, Spirits, History, Ships). The Log contains "Hidden Tags" (e.g., Arcane, Drow, First Captain) tracked by the DM that unlock future transformations or ship upgrades.

### Discovery Tiers
1. **Local:** Small area (e.g., hidden cove). Grants 1 Lore, normal XP.
2. **Regional:** Changes travel/trade for a region (e.g., safe shipping lane). Grants 2 Lore, ship/port benefit.
3. **Major:** Matters to a major faction (e.g., lost civilization outpost). Grants 4 Lore, strong faction reaction, unlock.
4. **World-Shaping:** Changes the map or economy (e.g., Underdark ocean route). Grants 8 Lore, major political shift.
5. **Mythic:** Happens only in legend (e.g., First Captain revelation). Grants 12 Lore, capstone narrative effects.

## The Passage System
The ocean is the dungeon. Passages are the "rooms." A voyage consists of multiple Passages.
1. **Captain's Orders:** Set the priority (Fast, Survey, Hunt, Hidden, Trade, Deep).
2. **The Sea Responds:** DM determines weather, encounters, ruins, or monsters.
3. **The Crew Acts:** Navigators, lookouts, and specialists report.
4. **Player Decisions:** Fight, negotiate, hide, trade, board.
5. **Resolution:** Update supplies, repairs, Lore, and morale.

### Boarding Combat (The Corsair Dungeon)
A hostile ship is a moving dungeon. Boarding has phases: Close distance, Cannon exchange, Hook, Swing across, Deck battle, Officer duel, Capture, Salvage.

## Ship Construction Rules Engine
A ship is built using **Ship Construction Points (SCP)**.
- **Size 1 (Small Craft):** 50 SCP, 1 Hull slot. (Scout/Smuggler).
- **Size 2 (Light Vessel):** 100 SCP, 2 Hull slots. (Piracy/Fast attack).
- **Size 3 (Medium Vessel):** 200 SCP, 3 Hull slots. (Professional pirate ship).
- **Size 4 (Heavy Vessel):** 350 SCP, 5 Hull slots. (Frigate/Galleon).
- **Size 5 (Capital Vessel):** 600 SCP, 8 Hull slots. (Man-o-war).

**Categories & Materials:**
SCP is spent on Structure, Propulsion, Combat, Utility, and Special Systems. Weapons take up slots (e.g., Light Cannon = 1 slot, 5 SCP; Dragonfire Cannon = 5 slots, 75 SCP).
Materials modify Hull Strength, Weight, and Magical Affinity. (e.g., Ironwood adds +40% Hull, +20% Weight; Darkwood adds +15% Hull, -30% Weight).

Ships can install Magical (teleportation anchors), Psionic (thought navigation), Divine (celestial sails), or Spirit (living figureheads) systems.


---

# Chapter 7: Dungeon Master's Guide

A Wayfinder campaign should feel like routes opening, rivals responding, nations adjusting, and legends forming. The DM's role is not to restrict the Wayfinder's influence, but to ensure that influence creates new challenges and opportunities.

## Running the Campaign
- **Naval Campaigns:** Track positioning, morale, supplies, and weather. Captain's Techniques create tactical advantages, not automatic victory.
- **Exploration Campaigns:** Exploration techniques should reveal clues and paths, but they do not eliminate the discovery process. Discovering a new route creates story (e.g., rival explorers, ancient guardians).
- **Political Campaigns:** First Captain techniques create influence and reputation, not mind control. The captain can gain audiences or secure trade agreements, but kings do not automatically surrender their thrones.
- **Fleet Warfare:** Fleet conflicts are strategic encounters. Ask: Who has superior intelligence? Who controls positioning? What are the objectives? 

## Handling Consequences
Every major achievement creates reactions.
- **Immediate Effect:** What changes now?
- **Local Response:** Who notices? (e.g., local pirates, merchants).
- **Regional Response:** Who reacts? (e.g., Crimson Dominion investigates, Drow launch a raid).
- **Long-Term Consequence:** How does the world change? (e.g., a new era of exploration begins).

### Tracking the World
- **Captain Reputation:** Fame, fear, and political influence.
- **Maritime Relations:** Allies, enemies, and neutral factions.
- **Known Routes:** Safe paths, dangers, and discoveries.

## Exploit Prevention & Rules Clarifications
To preserve the campaign sandbox and prevent the Wayfinder from bypassing all gameplay:

1. **Fleet + First Captain Interaction:** 
   - *Rule:* Fleet techniques affect tactical command during active operations. First Captain techniques affect strategic influence. A creature can benefit from only **one** Fleet technique command effect at a time.
2. **Mystic/Hunter Information Limits:**
   - *Rule:* Information gained through techniques provides direction, clues, and probabilities. It does not automatically reveal all hidden creatures, secret motives, or bypass investigation. Perfect information destroys mystery.
3. **Corsair Siege Limits:**
   - *Rule:* Siege-related techniques create openings and tactical advantages. They do not automatically destroy structures, ships, or magical defenses. You create a breach; the fortress does not instantly collapse.
4. **Legendary World Effects:**
   - *Rule:* Permanent world-changing effects (e.g., discovering impossible routes, establishing sovereign domains) require time, resources, and narrative consequences. The captain can influence history, but they do not own it.
5. **Passive & Action Economy Limits:**
   - *Rule:* Bonuses from Captain's Techniques follow normal D&D 3.5 stacking rules. A Wayfinder may use only **one** Immediate-Action Captain's Technique per round.

## Final DM Principle
*The captain may change the world, but the world always responds. A Wayfinder's greatest victories should not close the campaign; they should create the next chapter.*

Captain’s Techniques

Player Guide: Building Your Captain

Wayfinder Sourcebook - Character Development Chapter

Chapter Purpose

Captain’s Techniques define how a Wayfinder grows from a capable traveler into a legendary captain.

A Wayfinder does not advance by simply collecting stronger attacks or larger numerical bonuses. The system is designed around identity:

What kind of captain are you?

How do you solve problems?

What does your crew believe you represent?

How does the world change because you are present?

Captain’s Techniques use seven categories and four progression tiers:

Categories define identity.
Tiers define growth.

The approved categories are:

Seamanship

Duelist

Corsair

Fleet

Hunter

Mystic

First Captain

The approved tiers are:

Least

Lesser

Greater

Legendary

The subsystem is designed as an invocation-style modular advancement system comparable in structure to Warlock Invocations, but with a different identity: the Wayfinder masters opportunity, position, logistics, exploration, command, and influence. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Section 1: Choosing Your Captain Identity

Before selecting individual techniques, determine what kind of captain your Wayfinder is becoming.

A captain identity answers:

What do you command?

What problems do you specialize in solving?

What kind of legend will people tell?

Your identity is shaped by:

Primary Category

Secondary Category

Technique Tier Choices

Campaign Role

A captain focused on exploration will make different choices than a captain building a fleet or ruling a pirate kingdom.

Section 2: Understanding Categories

Categories represent different philosophies of captaincy.

Choosing a category does not lock a character into one role forever. It establishes the foundation of advancement.

A captain may begin as a sailor, become a raider, and eventually become a ruler.

The important question is:

What is the core of your captain’s legend?

Section 3: Understanding Tiers

Least Techniques

Identity:

"The captain begins."

Least techniques represent the foundation of your captain identity.

These abilities establish:

personal style,

basic mastery,

early specialization.

Expected Power:

Least techniques are practical abilities.

They should:

solve common problems,

define your role,

provide reliable advantages.

Gameplay Purpose:

Least techniques answer:

"What kind of captain are you becoming?"

Examples:

the explorer who never loses the trail,

the duelist who wins impossible fights,

the corsair who breaks enemy defenses,

the navigator who reads dangerous waters.

Lesser Techniques

Identity:

"The captain becomes recognized."

Lesser techniques represent specialization.

At this stage, the captain is no longer merely skilled.

They are known for something.

Expected Power:

Lesser techniques provide:

stronger specialization,

expanded capability,

improved control over situations.

Gameplay Purpose:

Lesser techniques answer:

"What kind of captain have you become?"

This is where characters begin strongly expressing their chosen path.

Greater Techniques

Identity:

"The captain becomes a major force."

Greater techniques represent advanced mastery.

The captain is no longer reacting to events.

They influence them.

Expected Power:

Greater techniques provide:

encounter-changing effects,

strategic advantages,

advanced command options.

Gameplay Purpose:

Greater techniques answer:

"What situations can you control?"

These abilities should shape battles, expeditions, and major conflicts without replacing the campaign itself. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Legendary Techniques

Identity:

"The captain becomes history."

Legendary techniques represent campaign-scale influence.

These abilities belong to captains whose names are known beyond their own ship.

Expected Power:

Legendary techniques may influence:

fleets,

factions,

territories,

political situations,

major voyages.

Gameplay Purpose:

Legendary techniques answer:

"What changes because you exist?"

Legendary abilities create consequences. They do not remove the need for adventure.

The strongest captain is not the one who avoids consequences.

The strongest captain is the one whose victories create challenges worthy of their legend. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Captain’s Techniques

Dungeon Master Integration Guide

Wayfinder Sourcebook

DM Resource Chapter

Introduction: Running a Captain’s Campaign

Captain’s Techniques are designed around a different style of advancement than traditional class features.

A traditional adventurer asks:

“How do I overcome this challenge?”

A Wayfinder captain asks:

“How does overcoming this challenge change the voyage?”

Captain’s Techniques are intended to influence:

naval campaigns,

exploration campaigns,

pirate conflicts,

political campaigns,

fleet warfare,

island kingdoms,

supernatural oceans.

The DM’s role is not to limit Captain’s Techniques. The role is to allow those abilities to create meaningful consequences.

A successful Captain’s Techniques campaign should show:

routes opening,

rivals adapting,

factions responding,

territories changing,

legends forming.

The subsystem’s core philosophy is that power creates opportunities and consequences rather than removing the need for adventure. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Section 1: Managing Captain Abilities

1.1 Evaluating Powerful Abilities

Captain’s Techniques differ from ordinary abilities because many affect the world around the character.

A DM should evaluate abilities using three questions:

1. What problem does the ability solve?

Examples:

A Seamanship technique may solve:

dangerous navigation,

difficult travel,

environmental hazards.

A Fleet technique may solve:

coordination problems,

command issues,

battlefield organization.

A First Captain technique may solve:

organizational limitations,

diplomatic barriers,

logistical challenges.

The question is not:

“Does this ability bypass the adventure?”

The question is:

“What new situation does this ability create?”

1.2 Maintaining Player Agency

Captain’s Techniques should increase player agency.

They should allow players to:

choose routes,

influence factions,

command resources,

establish reputations,

change strategic situations.

They should not remove:

meaningful choices,

consequences,

opposing interests,

campaign uncertainty.

Example:

A Mystic Navigator discovers a hidden sea route.

Incorrect interpretation:

“The party automatically wins every voyage.”

Correct interpretation:

“The party gains access to a route others cannot use. Now merchants, pirates, kingdoms, and ancient guardians care about that route.”

The ability creates story pressure.

It does not end the story.

1.3 Avoiding Removed Choices

The primary DM warning:

Do not convert influence into certainty.

Captain’s Techniques should not become:

automatic diplomacy,

automatic victory,

perfect information,

unlimited resources.

The subsystem balance audit specifically identifies this concern:

Fleet should affect battle, not replace military campaigns.

First Captain should affect organizations, not create automatic obedience.

Mystic and Hunter should provide information, not absolute knowledge.

Corsair should create openings, not automatic destruction. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Section 2: Legendary Techniques

Legendary Techniques represent the point where the Wayfinder becomes a historical figure.

They affect:

voyages,

fleets,

nations,

organizations,

maritime power.

They are not simply stronger combat abilities.

They are campaign tools.

2.1 Handling Legendary Effects

When a Legendary Technique is used, consider:

Immediate Effect

What changes right now?

Examples:

a route becomes accessible,

a fleet gains an advantage,

a faction recognizes the captain,

a supernatural boundary is crossed.

Secondary Effects

Who notices?

Possible reactions:

governments investigate,

pirates attempt exploitation,

merchants seek agreements,

enemies create countermeasures,

supernatural beings respond.

Long-Term Effects

How does the world change?

Track:

reputation,

territory,

alliances,

enemies,

economic changes,

political consequences.

Legendary abilities are designed to create lasting campaign developments. Permanent world-changing effects require time, resources, and campaign integration. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

2.2 Faction Responses

Factions should react according to their interests.

Merchants

Possible responses:

request access,

offer contracts,

compete,

attempt control.

Pirates

Possible responses:

challenge the captain,

recruit the captain,

imitate the captain,

sabotage the captain.

Governments

Possible responses:

negotiate,

monitor,

regulate,

attempt alliance.

Supernatural Powers

Possible responses:

curiosity,

hostility,

bargains,

ancient claims.

A Legendary captain becomes a strategic factor.

The world notices.

2.3 Reputation Changes

Reputation should evolve over time.

Recommended progression:

Local Reputation

The captain becomes known among:

crews,

ports,

local factions.

Regional Reputation

The captain becomes recognized by:

kingdoms,

major organizations,

rival captains.

Legendary Reputation

The captain becomes a figure of maritime history.

Possible results:

songs,

myths,

political influence,

historical records.

Section 3: Fleet-Level Play

3.1 Multiple Ships

Fleet-level campaigns require tracking more than individual combat.

Important factors:

objectives,

positioning,

supplies,

morale,

command structure,

weather,

enemy strategy.

Avoid reducing fleet warfare to:

“Roll attacks until ships explode.”

Ships represent:

resources,

organizations,

strategic positions.

3.2 Organizations

Fleet techniques interact with organizations.

Track:

command hierarchy,

officers,

specialists,

resources,

loyalty,

reputation.

A fleet is not just a number.

It is a living structure.

3.3 Strategic Conflicts

Fleet conflicts should include objectives.

Examples:

Victory Conditions:

capture a harbor,

protect a convoy,

escape pursuit,

blockade an enemy,

establish dominance.

Not every naval conflict requires destruction.

A smarter captain wins the objective.

3.4 Territory Control

Territory should be tracked as a campaign resource.

Examples:

Controlled:

ports,

trade routes,

islands,

safe harbors,

supply chains.

Contested:

pirate waters,

disputed colonies,

supernatural zones.

Lost:

captured ports,

blocked routes,

hostile territory.

Section 4: Narrative Consequences

Captain’s Techniques are designed to change campaign states.

The DM should maintain:

NPC reactions,

faction relationships,

reputation,

territory,

route control.

4.1 NPC Reactions

NPC reactions depend on:

the captain’s reputation,

past actions,

current interests.

Examples:

A feared Corsair Captain may receive:

fear,

hostility,

reluctant respect.

A First Captain may receive:

diplomatic attention,

political requests,

strategic opposition.

4.2 Political Changes

Political effects should create negotiations, not automatic control.

A First Captain does not simply say:

“You obey.”

Instead:

“You must now consider this captain a major political actor.”

Possible outcomes:

treaties,

rival coalitions,

trade agreements,

political opposition.

The balance review specifically establishes:

First Captain creates influence, not automatic obedience. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

4.3 Reputation Systems

Track:

Allies

Who supports the captain?

Rivals

Who challenges the captain?

Enemies

Who benefits from the captain failing?

Dependents

Who relies on the captain?

Power creates relationships.

Relationships create stories.

4.4 Changing World States

When a captain succeeds, update the campaign.

Examples:

New Route Discovered

Changes:

trade,

piracy,

exploration,

political value.

Pirate Fleet Created

Changes:

naval balance,

rival responses,

territorial claims.

Supernatural Ocean Mastery

Changes:

magical interest,

ancient awakenings,

unusual threats.

The world should remember.

Campaign Integration Guidelines

Naval Campaigns

Emphasize:

ships,

crews,

routes,

battles,

maritime reputation.

Captain’s Techniques should influence how naval situations are approached.

Exploration Campaigns

Emphasize:

unknown locations,

lost civilizations,

impossible terrain.

Exploration abilities should reveal opportunities, clues, and paths.

They should not remove discovery.

Pirate Campaigns

Emphasize:

reputation,

rival captains,

raids,

alliances,

territory.

Political Campaigns

Emphasize:

diplomacy,

organizations,

influence,

competing interests.

Supernatural Ocean Campaigns

Emphasize:

strange routes,

magical seas,

ancient powers,

impossible voyages.

Balance Considerations

Fleet + First Captain

Potential Risk:

Military and political authority combining into automatic victory.

Correction:

Fleet controls tactical operations.

First Captain controls influence and organization.

Neither creates automatic success. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Mystic + Hunter

Potential Risk:

Perfect information.

Correction:

Information provides:

clues,

direction,

probabilities.

It does not reveal everything.

Corsair + Siege

Potential Risk:

Automatic destruction.

Correction:

Creates:

openings,

vulnerabilities,

advantages.

It does not instantly destroy defenses.

Legendary Influence

Potential Risk:

Permanent effects accumulating without limitation.

Correction:

Major changes require:

time,

resources,

consequences,

campaign tracking.

Exploit Review

Exploit Status

No fundamental mechanical exploits identified.

The primary risks are interpretation issues.

Resolved Issues

Issue
Resolution
Fleet stacking
Similar command effects do not stack unless stated
Perfect information
Information remains incomplete
Automatic siege victory
Siege creates openings only
Political domination
Influence does not equal control
Legendary bypass
Major effects require consequences

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Final DM Assessment

Captain’s Techniques are approved for:

naval campaigns,

exploration campaigns,

political campaigns,

pirate campaigns,

kingdom-building campaigns,

supernatural maritime adventures.

The subsystem is not designed to make the captain unstoppable.

It is designed to make the captain important.

The fighter defeats the enemy.

The wizard changes the battlefield.

The Wayfinder changes the voyage.

Captain’s Techniques

Dungeon Master Appendix

Wayfinder Sourcebook

Running Captain-Scale Campaigns

Captain’s Techniques are designed to expand the scope of play beyond individual encounters.

A Wayfinder captain does not only defeat enemies. They:

discover routes,

command vessels,

influence factions,

establish reputations,

reshape maritime conflicts,

create new strategic possibilities.

The Dungeon Master’s responsibility is not to reduce these abilities. It is to place them into a living world where meaningful choices continue to exist.

The subsystem is designed for:

naval campaigns,

exploration campaigns,

pirate campaigns,

political campaigns,

island kingdom campaigns,

supernatural maritime adventures. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

The guiding principle:

Captain’s Techniques modify situations. They do not replace campaigns. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Chapter 1: Running Captain’s Techniques

Understanding Captain Abilities

Captain’s Techniques represent increasing levels of maritime mastery.

They progress through:

Tier
Captain Identity
Campaign Role
Least
Emerging Captain
Personal mastery
Lesser
Established Captain
Specialized expertise
Greater
Master Captain
Strategic influence
Legendary
Historic Captain
Campaign-scale impact

The DM should treat these abilities as tools that create opportunities.

A successful Captain’s Technique use should usually produce:

An advantage.

A new option.

A changed situation.

A meaningful consequence.

It should not produce:

automatic victory,

automatic obedience,

perfect information,

removal of all opposition.

Chapter 2: DM Preparation Checklist

Before running a Captain-focused campaign, prepare:

Maritime Environment

Track:

known routes,

dangerous waters,

ports,

islands,

trade lanes,

supernatural locations.

Factions

Track:

allies,

enemies,

rivals,

organizations,

political interests.

Reputation

Track how the world views the captain:

Examples:

unknown sailor,

respected captain,

feared pirate,

legendary maritime figure.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Territory

Track:

controlled ports,

safe harbors,

contested waters,

trade routes,

strategic locations.

Resources

Track:

ships,

crews,

supplies,

alliances,

political leverage.

Chapter 3: Naval Campaigns

Running Naval Adventures

Naval adventures should not become simple ship-to-ship attack exchanges.

A naval encounter should have:

Objective

Examples:

capture a vessel,

escape pursuit,

protect a convoy,

discover an island,

break a blockade.

Obstacles

Examples:

storms,

reefs,

enemy commanders,

political restrictions,

supernatural hazards.

Captain's Technique Impact

Ask:

“What advantage does this technique create?”

Examples:

A Seamanship ability may provide:

safer navigation,

improved positioning,

better route access.

A Fleet ability may provide:

coordination,

formation advantages,

improved battlefield control.

The remaining conflict still exists.

The captain changed the situation.

They did not erase it. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Chapter 4: Exploration Campaigns

Exploration Philosophy

Exploration Captain’s Techniques accelerate discovery.

They do not remove discovery.

The DM should maintain:

Known Routes

Reliable paths:

trade routes,

mapped waters,

established passages.

Unknown Regions

Areas requiring:

investigation,

navigation,

survival,

experimentation.

Discoveries

Examples:

lost civilizations,

hidden islands,

ancient ruins,

magical locations.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Information Boundaries

Mystic and Hunter abilities provide:

clues,

direction,

opportunities,

useful observations.

They do not provide:

every secret,

every trap,

every hidden creature,

perfect answers.

The subsystem specifically identifies information abilities as a potential exploit area and limits them to useful information rather than certainty. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Chapter 5: Pirate Conflicts

Pirate campaigns revolve around:

reputation,

rival captains,

territory,

resources,

alliances.

A Corsair-focused Wayfinder changes the balance of piracy.

The DM should track:

Pirate Reputation

Questions:

Who fears the captain?

Who respects the captain?

Who wants the captain removed?

Rival Captains

A legendary pirate should attract:

challengers,

imitators,

alliances,

betrayals.

Pirate Politics

A pirate fleet is not automatically loyal.

It has:

ambitions,

personalities,

rivalries,

competing goals.

Chapter 6: Fleet Warfare

Resolving Fleet-Level Actions

Fleet conflicts should be treated as strategic encounters.

Track:

Fleet Strength

Includes:

number of ships,

ship quality,

crew experience,

commanders,

supplies.

Tactical Position

Includes:

wind advantage,

terrain,

formations,

surprise,

intelligence.

Political Situation

Includes:

allies,

enemies,

treaties,

reputation.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Using Fleet Techniques

Fleet abilities influence:

coordination,

timing,

positioning,

effectiveness.

They do not remove:

planning,

risk,

leadership decisions.

Example:

Before:

An enemy fleet has numerical superiority.

After:

The captain creates:

improved formation,

better positioning,

increased survival odds.

The battle remains.

The captain changed the odds.

Chapter 7: Managing First Captain Influence

First Captain Philosophy

First Captain represents:

authority,

diplomacy,

organization,

logistics,

reputation,

strategic influence.

It does not represent mind control.

The core distinction:

Fleet makes people fight better.
First Captain makes people follow.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Political Influence Rules

A First Captain ability may create:

alliances,

negotiations,

organizations,

reputation,

opportunities.

It does not create:

automatic loyalty,

instant obedience,

permanent political control.

A king may respect a legendary captain without surrendering the kingdom.

A pirate council may fear a captain without becoming devoted followers.

Chapter 8: Island Kingdom Campaigns

Captain’s Techniques can support campaigns involving:

island nations,

maritime empires,

pirate kingdoms,

trade networks.

Track:

Territory

Examples:

Controlled:

ports,

islands,

harbors.

Contested:

disputed waters,

enemy claims.

Unclaimed:

unexplored regions.

Infrastructure

Track:

shipyards,

supply chains,

trade routes,

alliances.

Political Impact

A captain’s actions may change:

borders,

economies,

diplomacy,

military balance.

These changes should create new campaign situations.

Chapter 9: Supernatural Oceans

Mystic and Legendary Captain abilities may interact with:

magical seas,

impossible routes,

ancient powers,

supernatural storms.

The DM should preserve mystery.

A supernatural navigation ability should reveal:

paths,

warnings,

possibilities.

It should not reveal:

every danger,

every secret,

every solution.

Chapter 10: Legendary Techniques

Legendary Techniques represent campaign-scale abilities.

They may influence:

voyages,

fleets,

territories,

nations,

exploration,

naval warfare,

political structures.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Managing Legendary Effects

When a Legendary Technique is used:

Ask:

Immediate Impact

What changes now?

Strategic Impact

Who reacts?

Examples:

governments,

merchants,

pirates,

supernatural entities.

Long-Term Consequence

What changes permanently?

Examples:

new routes,

new alliances,

new enemies,

new territories.

Legendary Limit

Permanent world changes require:

time,

resources,

narrative consequences.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Recommended reputation progression:

Stage
Meaning
Unknown Sailor
Local recognition
Respected Captain
Regional reputation
Famous Captain
Major influence
Living Legend
Historical importance

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Track:

allies,

rivals,

enemies,

dependents.

Chapter 12: Faction Tracking

For every major faction record:

Goals

What does the faction want?

Resources

What can they provide?

Relationship

Current status:

hostile,

neutral,

cooperative,

allied.

Response

How do they react to the captain’s growth?

Chapter 13: Route Tracking

Maintain a maritime map.

Track:

Known Routes

Safe travel.

Hidden Routes

Recently discovered paths.

Lost Routes

Ancient or inaccessible passages.

Controlled Routes

Strategic resources.

A captain who changes navigation changes the world.

Common Rulings Guide

Fleet + First Captain

Potential issue:

Military and political authority combining into automatic victory.

Resolution:

Fleet affects:

battles,

formations,

tactical operations.

First Captain affects:

organizations,

authority,

influence.

Neither grants automatic success.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Mystic + Hunter

Potential issue:

Perfect information.

Resolution:

Provides:

clues,

direction,

advantages.

Not:

absolute knowledge.

Corsair + Siege

Potential issue:

Automatic destruction.

Resolution:

Creates weaknesses.

Does not automatically destroy:

ships,

walls,

fortifications.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Fleet Stacking

Resolution:

A creature can benefit from only one Fleet command effect at a time.

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Campaign Integration Examples

Example 1: The Lost Route

Explorer Captain discovers a forgotten passage.

Immediate:

The party gains a travel advantage.

Consequences:

merchants want access,

pirates want control,

kingdoms want influence.

Example 2: The Pirate Fleet

Corsair Captain defeats a rival fleet.

Immediate:

The captain gains reputation.

Consequences:

survivors seek revenge,

pirates negotiate,

governments respond.

Example 3: The Maritime Kingdom

First Captain establishes authority.

Immediate:

The captain gains political influence.

Consequences:

rivals appear,

treaties form,

enemies organize.

Balance Review

Overall Assessment

PASS

The subsystem’s main balance risks are not raw numerical power.

They are:

scope interpretation,

information certainty,

political control,

fleet stacking.

The approved corrections preserve power while maintaining campaign play. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Exploit Review

Exploit 1: Fleet + First Captain

Status:

Resolved.

Correction:

Separate tactical command from strategic influence.

Exploit 2: Mystic + Hunter

Status:

Resolved.

Correction:

Information provides clues, not certainty.

Exploit 3: Corsair Siege

Status:

Resolved.

Correction:

Create openings, not automatic destruction.

Exploit 4: Legendary Influence Chain

Status:

Resolved.

Correction:

Permanent changes require:

downtime,

resources,

consequences.

The captain now has a rulebook for changing the world without accidentally deleting the campaign. A surprisingly difficult standard for fictional people who insist on naming ships after storms and then being shocked when storms happen.


## NPC Captains and Villain Builds
To seamlessly integrate Captain's Techniques into your campaign, you can assign them to notable NPCs and recurring villains.

### Low-Level Rival: The Upstart Corsair (CR 4)
- **Role:** Aggressive privateer or early campaign antagonist.
- **Techniques:** Find the Weak Board (Least Corsair), Sea Legs (Least Seamanship).
- **Tactics:** Relies on aggressive boarding actions and environmental hazards to overwhelm the party's ship.

### Mid-Level Antagonist: The Fleet Commander (CR 10)
- **Role:** Leader of a regional naval force or pirate armada.
- **Techniques:** Crew Rotation (Least Fleet), Captain's Challenge (Least Duelist), plus Lesser Fleet techniques.
- **Tactics:** Engages at scale, using formations and coordinated bombardments before closing for a dramatic duel.

### Legendary Villain: The Immortal Pirate King (CR 19)
- **Role:** Campaign-ending threat with world-shaping influence.
- **Techniques:** Reserve the Hold (Least First Captain), Whispering Compass (Least Mystic), plus Greater and Legendary Corsair/First Captain techniques.
- **Tactics:** Operates on a strategic level, controlling factions and impossible routes. Rarely fights directly unless backed by overwhelming supernatural support.

## Treasure Considerations
When awarding treasure to a party with a Wayfinder, consider items that enhance their chosen techniques rather than just raw stats. For example, a spyglass that grants bonus uses of *Chart the Unknown* or an enchanted cutlass that augments the *Captain's Challenge* technique.

Scope

This document defines the visual direction for the Captain’s Techniques chapter.

It establishes:

illustration requirements,

placement,

visual hierarchy,

art style,

reference graphics.

It does not introduce:

mechanics,

rules changes,

new techniques,

additional subsystem content.

The goal is to visually communicate the evolution of a Wayfinder from skilled sailor into legendary captain.

2. Chapter Splash Art

Asset: “The Captain’s Horizon”

Placement

Opening chapter spread.

Format

Full-page or two-page illustration.

Description

A legendary Wayfinder captain stands at the helm of a weathered vessel.

The scene should include:

towering waves,

distant islands,

storm clouds,

rival ships on the horizon,

mysterious lights beneath the ocean surface,

a crew working behind the captain.

The captain should appear as:

explorer,

commander,

warrior,

leader.

Not merely a pirate.

Visual Purpose

Communicate:

“Captain’s Techniques are about changing the world through mastery of the sea.”

Each category receives a visual identity piece.

Half-page illustration or full-page divider.

dangerous reefs,

Overall Style

dark fantasy,

Color Direction

Lighting

Preferred:

dramatic skies,

lantern light,

moonlit seas,

magical glow effects.

Avoid:

bright cartoon colors,

clean modern aesthetics.

Character Design

experienced,

capable,

distinct.

Avoid:

generic pirates,

random sailors,

exaggerated comedy.

Ship Design

Ships should communicate progression.

Early:

practical vessels.

Advanced:

decorated command ships.

Legendary:

iconic vessels recognized across the world.

Typography Integration

chapter titles,

rules boxes,

sidebars.

Phase 1 should be divided into 6 Command Prompts.
Reasoning:
A single “Complete Sourcebook Manuscript Assembly” prompt is too large because
it requires:
rules framework
category explanations
tier explanations
all technique entries
formatting
cross-reference tables
publication cleanup
Trying to force all of that through one generation would create the classic sourcebook
problem: the ﬁrst half gets lovingly polished while the back half becomes a tired
goblin scribbling “see previous page.” Splitting it preserves consistency and allows
audits between stages.

Captain’s Techniques
Category and Tier Chapter
Captain’s Techniques are the Wayﬁnder’s deﬁning advancement system. They are not
just tricks, stunts, or combat riders. They are the means by which a Wayﬁnder
becomes a captain in the full sense of the word: a person who can shape voyages,
crews, battles, and the fortunes of the sea.
The source material establishes the seven oﬃcial categories and the four-tier
progression of the subsystem. It also establishes that the chapter should function as
player-facing rules text, with clear category identities, clear tier identities, and enough
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 54 / 434

guidance for both character building and campaign use. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure 
Setup 2.txt
Category Chapter
1. Seamanship
Seamanship is the category of naval mastery. It covers ship operation, navigation,
environmental interaction, and voyage leadership.
A Wayﬁnder who leans into Seamanship is the captain who understands the sea as a
living route system. They know how to move a vessel, read conditions, survive hostile
waters, and keep a voyage on course when lesser sailors would be lost, wrecked, or
tempted to panic. Seamanship is the category for characters who want to make travel
itself matter.
In play, Seamanship supports:
navigation and routeﬁnding,
ship handling and movement,
survival in dangerous waters,
environmental adaptation,
leadership during voyages.
2. Duelist
Duelist is the category of personal combat mastery. It covers captain-versus-captain
conﬂicts, precision combat, and tactical superiority.
A Duelist Wayﬁnder is the captain who settles hard questions personally. This category
is for characters who want to dominate challenges through skill, timing, and presence
rather than through sheer brute force. Duelist does not replace battleﬁeld command.
It wins the knife-ﬁght at the rail, the duel on the quarterdeck, and the decisive one-on-
one moment when a rival commander must be answered directly.
In play, Duelist supports:
personal combat,
oﬃcer duels,
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 55 / 434

precision attacks,
counters and combat timing,
tactical superiority in close engagements.
3. Corsair
Corsair is the category of piracy, boarding actions, intimidation, and unconventional
warfare.
A Corsair Wayﬁnder ﬁghts dirty in the most honorable way possible, which is to say,
the way that keeps the ship aﬂoat and the enemy regretting their life choices. This
category represents breaching, raiding, disruption, and the art of breaking an
opponent’s conﬁdence and defensive structure without needing to play fair about it.
In play, Corsair supports:
boarding actions,
ship raids,
intimidation,
breaching and disruption,
unconventional tactics against fortiﬁed targets.
4. Fleet
Fleet is the category of command structures, multiple vessels, strategic warfare, and
naval campaigns.
A Fleet Wayﬁnder is not primarily a lone hero. They are an organizer of coordinated
violence and disciplined action. Fleet is the category for captains who think in
formations, signal chains, squad roles, and operations that involve more than one ship
or more than one piece of the battleﬁeld.
In play, Fleet supports:
command structures,
allied coordination,
multiple vessels,
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 56 / 434

strategic warfare,
naval campaigns and battle planning.
5. Hunter
Hunter is the category of tracking, pursuit, monster hunting, and hostile-environment
survival.
A Hunter Wayﬁnder is the captain who does not let prey vanish just because it has the
good taste to run. This category covers the chase, the trail, the interception, and the
hard practical skill of surviving and operating in places that were not built to be
pleasant.
In play, Hunter supports:
tracking,
pursuit,
interception,
monster hunting,
survival in hostile terrain and dangerous waters.
6. Mystic
Mystic is the category of supernatural navigation, magical oceans, hidden knowledge,
and arcane inﬂuence.
A Mystic Wayﬁnder does not merely travel the sea. They negotiate with the sea’s
deeper truths. Mystic is the category for captains whose routeﬁnding depends on
omens, strange laws, symbolic insight, or direct supernatural interaction with the
world of water, weather, and hidden distance.
In play, Mystic supports:
supernatural navigation,
magical and storm-driven environments,
hidden knowledge,
arcane or omen-based inﬂuence,
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 57 / 434

exploration that goes beyond ordinary cartography.
7. First Captain
First Captain is the category of legendary leadership, campaign-scale inﬂuence, and
world-changing abilities.
This category is the capstone identity of a Wayﬁnder who has become more than a
shipmaster or even a war leader. First Captain abilities deal with organization,
logistics, diplomacy, reputation, authority, and the kind of inﬂuence that changes how
other people and institutions respond to the captain’s existence.
In play, First Captain supports:
legendary leadership,
campaign-scale inﬂuence,
logistics and organization,
diplomacy,
reputation and authority.
The published balance notes show that First Captain remains powerful but must not
become automatic obedience or universal control. The category creates inﬂuence, not
mind control, and should be handled as strategic authority rather than narrative
erasure. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup

useful,

🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 59 / 434

DM Interpretation Notes
The DM should treat Captain’s Techniques as campaign-facing, not just combat-facing.
The DM guidance established several important principles:
techniques provide advantages, not universal success,
exploration techniques provide information and direction, not perfect revelation,
naval abilities alter conditions and probabilities, not instant victory,
Legendary eﬀects create lasting consequences,
First Captain abilities create inﬂuence, not mind control. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure 
Setup 2.txt
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 62 / 434

Use these principles when adjudicating the subsystem:
Seamanship should make routes possible or safer, not erase travel challenges.
Duelist should reward precision and tempo, not replace every combat option.
Corsair should create openings and vulnerabilities, not automatically destroy ships
or fortiﬁcations.
Fleet should improve coordination, not let one captain dictate the entire war by
existing near it.
Hunter should provide clues, pursuit, and pressure, not omniscience.
Mystic should provide supernatural direction, not perfect answers.
First Captain should move factions, organizations, and reputations, not overwrite
free will.
The published balance notes also support strict limits on information, command
stacking, and narrative scope. Fleet beneﬁts should not stack freely, and information
techniques must remain directional rather than absolute. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure 
Setup 2.txt ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Captain’s Techniques
Player Guide: Building Your Captain
Wayﬁnder Sourcebook - Character Development Chapter
Chapter Purpose
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 66 / 434

Captain’s Techniques deﬁne how a Wayﬁnder grows from a capable traveler into a
legendary captain.
A Wayﬁnder does not advance by simply collecting stronger attacks or larger
numerical bonuses. The system is designed around identity:
What kind of captain are you?
How do you solve problems?
What does your crew believe you represent?
How does the world change because you are present?
Captain’s Techniques use seven categories and four progression tiers:
Categories deﬁne identity.
Tiers deﬁne growth.
The approved categories are:
Seamanship
Duelist
Corsair
Fleet
Hunter
Mystic
First Captain
The approved tiers are:
Least
Lesser
Greater
Legendary
The subsystem is designed as an invocation-style modular advancement system

Section 1: Choosing Your Captain Identity
Before selecting individual techniques, determine what kind of captain your Wayﬁnder
is becoming.
A captain identity answers:
What do you command?
What problems do you specialize in solving?
What kind of legend will people tell?
Your identity is shaped by:
1. Primary Category
2. Secondary Category
3. Technique Tier Choices
4. Campaign Role
A captain focused on exploration will make diﬀerent choices than a captain building a
ﬂeet or ruling a pirate kingdom.
Section 2: Understanding Categories
Categories represent diﬀerent philosophies of captaincy.
Choosing a category does not lock a character into one role forever. It establishes the
foundation of advancement.
A captain may begin as a sailor, become a raider, and eventually become a ruler.
The important question is:
What is the core of your captain’s legend?

Dungeon Master Integration Guide
Wayﬁnder Sourcebook
DM Resource Chapter
Introduction: Running a Captain’s Campaign
Captain’s Techniques are designed around a diﬀerent style of advancement than
traditional class features.
A traditional adventurer asks:
“How do I overcome this challenge?”
A Wayﬁnder captain asks:
“How does overcoming this challenge change the voyage?”
Captain’s Techniques are intended to inﬂuence:
naval campaigns,
exploration campaigns,
pirate conﬂicts,
political campaigns,
ﬂeet warfare,
island kingdoms,
supernatural oceans.
The DM’s role is not to limit Captain’s Techniques. The role is to allow those abilities to
create meaningful consequences.
A successful Captain’s Techniques campaign should show:
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 89 / 434

routes opening,
rivals adapting,
factions responding,
territories changing,
legends forming.
The subsystem’s core philosophy is that power creates opportunities and
consequences rather than removing the need for adventure. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure 
Setup 2.txt
Section 1: Managing Captain Abilities
1.1 Evaluating Powerful Abilities
Captain’s Techniques diﬀer from ordinary abilities because many aﬀect the world
around the character.
A DM should evaluate abilities using three questions:
1. What problem does the ability solve?
Examples:
A Seamanship technique may solve:
dangerous navigation,
diﬃcult travel,
environmental hazards.
A Fleet technique may solve:
coordination problems,
command issues,
battleﬁeld organization.
A First Captain technique may solve:
organizational limitations,
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 90 / 434

diplomatic barriers,
logistical challenges.
The question is not:
“Does this ability bypass the adventure?”
The question is:
“What new situation does this ability create?”
1.2 Maintaining Player Agency
Captain’s Techniques should increase player agency.
They should allow players to:
choose routes,
inﬂuence factions,
command resources,
establish reputations,
change strategic situations.
They should not remove:
meaningful choices,
consequences,
opposing interests,
campaign uncertainty.
Example:
A Mystic Navigator discovers a hidden sea route.
Incorrect interpretation:
“The party automatically wins every voyage.”
Correct interpretation:
“The party gains access to a route others cannot use. Now merchants, pirates,
kingdoms, and ancient guardians care about that route.”
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 91 / 434

The ability creates story pressure.
It does not end the story.
1.3 Avoiding Removed Choices
The primary DM warning:
Do not convert inﬂuence into certainty.
Captain’s Techniques should not become:
automatic diplomacy,
automatic victory,
perfect information,
unlimited resources.
The subsystem balance audit speciﬁcally identiﬁes this concern:
Fleet should aﬀect battle, not replace military campaigns.
First Captain should aﬀect organizations, not create automatic obedience.
Mystic and Hunter should provide information, not absolute knowledge.
Corsair should create openings, not automatic destruction. ChatGPT-Pirate 
Adventure Setup 2.txt
Section 2: Legendary Techniques
Legendary Techniques represent the point where the Wayﬁnder becomes a historical
ﬁgure.
They aﬀect:
voyages,
ﬂeets,
nations,
organizations,
maritime power.
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 92 / 434

They are not simply stronger combat abilities.
They are campaign tools.

What changes right now?

a route becomes accessible,
a ﬂeet gains an advantage,
a faction recognizes the captain,
a supernatural boundary is crossed.
Secondary Eﬀects
Who notices?

governments investigate,
pirates attempt exploitation,
merchants seek agreements,
enemies create countermeasures,
supernatural beings respond.

reputation,
territory,
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 93 / 434

alliances,
enemies,
economic changes,
political consequences.

Captain’s Techniques
Building Your Wayﬁnder Captain
A Wayﬁnder is not deﬁned only by what they defeat.
A Wayﬁnder is deﬁned by:
where they can go,
who follows them,
what challenges they overcome,
and how the world changes because they arrived.
Captain’s Techniques represent the growth of a Wayﬁnder from skilled voyager into
legendary captain.
They represent mastery over:
navigation,
combat,
piracy,
ﬂeets,
exploration,
supernatural oceans,
political inﬂuence.
The Wayﬁnder does not simply become stronger.
The Wayﬁnder becomes important.
Section 1: What Captain’s Techniques Represent
Captain’s Techniques are specialized abilities that allow a Wayﬁnder to develop a
unique captain identity.
They diﬀer from ordinary class abilities because they represent choice-driven
specialization.
Two Wayﬁnders of the same level may become completely diﬀerent types of captains:

one may discover lost worlds,

Captain’s Techniques progress through four tiers.
Least Techniques

your style,

talented sailors,
rising captains,
promising leaders.

The Developing Captain

your reputation grows,
your methods become recognizable,
your role becomes clearer.

The Captain Becomes History

These abilities can aﬀect:

territories,
organizations,
the course of history.
They create consequences.

Captain’s Techniques
Dungeon Master Appendix
Wayﬁnder Sourcebook
Running Captain-Scale Campaigns
Captain’s Techniques are designed to expand the scope of play beyond individual
encounters.
A Wayﬁnder captain does not only defeat enemies. They:
discover routes,
command vessels,
inﬂuence factions,
establish reputations,
reshape maritime conﬂicts,
create new strategic possibilities.
The Dungeon Master’s responsibility is not to reduce these abilities. It is to place them
into a living world where meaningful choices continue to exist.
The subsystem is designed for:
naval campaigns,
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 171 / 434

exploration campaigns,
pirate campaigns,
political campaigns,
island kingdom campaigns,
supernatural maritime adventures. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt
The guiding principle:
Captain’s Techniques modify situations. They do not replace campaigns.
ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt
Chapter 1: Running Captain’s Techniques
Understanding Captain Abilities
Captain’s Techniques represent increasing levels of maritime mastery.
They progress through:
Tier Captain Identity Campaign Role
Least Emerging Captain Personal mastery
Lesser Established Captain Specialized expertise
Greater Master Captain Strategic inﬂuence
Legendary Historic Captain Campaign-scale impact
The DM should treat these abilities as tools that create opportunities.
A successful Captain’s Technique use should usually produce:
1. An advantage.
2. A new option.
3. A changed situation.
4. A meaningful consequence.
It should not produce:
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 172 / 434

automatic victory,
automatic obedience,
perfect information,
removal of all opposition.

Wayﬁnder Sourcebook Art Direction Document

Scope
This document deﬁnes the visual direction for the Captain’s Techniques chapter.
It establishes:
illustration requirements,
placement,
visual hierarchy,
art style,
reference graphics.
It does not introduce:
mechanics,
rules changes,
new techniques,
additional subsystem content.

Appendix: Running Captain’s Techniques
Campaigns
Introduction: The Captain Changes the World
Captain’s Techniques represent a progression beyond personal combat ability.
A Wayﬁnder’s inﬂuence expands through:
ships,
crews,
routes,
factions,
territories,
reputation,
supernatural maritime forces.
A DM should treat Captain’s Techniques as tools that reshape the campaign
environment.
The correct question is not:
“How do I stop this ability?”
The correct question is:
“What changes because this captain can do this?”
A captain discovering a new route does not end exploration. It creates:
trade opportunities,
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 295 / 434

rival expeditions,
political interest,
territorial conﬂicts.
A captain commanding a ﬂeet does not replace warfare. It changes the scale of
warfare.

Campaign Focus
Naval campaigns emphasize:
ships,
crews,
voyages,
naval battles,
maritime reputation.
Captain’s Techniques should inﬂuence:
ship operation,
tactical decisions,
survival,
crew eﬀectiveness.

supplies,
weather,
enemy tactics.
Fleet Scale

strategic conﬂicts,
naval campaigns.

🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 297 / 434

information,
paths forward.
They should not remove discovery.
Example

Immediate Result

ancient guardians awaken.

🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 299 / 434

bounty hunters arrive,
pirate factions challenge authority,
merchants seek protection.
Section IV: Fleet Warfare
Managing Fleet-Level Actions
Fleet warfare should operate on strategic decisions.

objectives,
positioning,
resources,
alliances,
enemy goals.
Avoiding the “Single Character Army” Problem
A high-level captain may command ﬂeets.

oﬃcers,
crews,
logistics,
political structures.
The captain directs the machine.
The captain is not the machine.

Section X: Common Rulings Guide
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 307 / 434

“Does a Captain’s Technique automatically succeed?”
No.
Captain’s Techniques provide advantages, not universal success.
“Can Legendary Techniques change the world?”
Yes.
They create lasting consequences.
“Can First Captain abilities force loyalty?”
No.
They create inﬂuence, not mind control.
“Can exploration abilities reveal everything?”
No.
They provide information and advantages.
“Can naval abilities instantly win battles?”
No.
They change conditions and probabilities.

Immediate Result

Short-Term Result
What changes next?
Long-Term Result
How does the world adapt?

This is deﬁnitely too large for one prompt if you want high-quality output. I'd split it
into 10 sequential production prompts. Each has a single responsibility,
minimizing context drift and making it easy to review before moving on. Humans
invented modular software engineering for a reason. It turns out the same trick works
on giant RPG books.
