Executive Summary
The campaign framework is strong at the premise level and thin at the atlas /
polity / culture level. The manuscript already gives you a playable high concept: a
shattered archipelago world, a discovery loop, major faction identities, and a strong
naval-fantasy tone. What is still missing is the infrastructure that makes the setting
feel lived-in: maps, borders, local economies, inter-faction diplomacy, regional
identities, settlement detail, and a historical timeline beyond the founding
catastrophe. The book has a compass. It does not yet have a full chart table.
Estimated campaign framework completion: 43%.
A. Completed worldbuilding
The following foundation is already in place:
Core setting premise: The world is The Shattered Expanse, broken into tens
of thousands of islands after the Sundering Tide, with only about 35% of the
oceans reliably charted and the rest called The Unwritten Sea.
Wayfinder_Sourcebook.pdf
Campaign identity: The setting is built around the loop Rumor → Expedition →
Discovery → Map → Route → Trade → Conﬂict, which gives the campaign a
coherent play structure. Wayfinder_Sourcebook.pdf
Major macro-themes: The manuscript already deﬁnes the Uncharted Ocean,
Great Magical Trade Network, Pirate Renaissance, Underdark Ocean, and
Lost Civilization Mystery as the setting’s main engines.
Wayfinder_Sourcebook.pdf
Core metaphysics: The four magic systems are clearly deﬁned: Arcane,
Psionics, Spirit, and Deep Magic. Wayfinder_Sourcebook.pdf
Faction anchor points: Six major factions are already named with home bases
and broad identities: Free Captains Alliance / Blackwake Harbor, Grand
Merchant Consortium / Port Meridian, Crimson Dominion / The Crimson
Citadel, Drowned Silk Houses / Nymorath Deep Harbor, Spirit Keepers /
The Last Harbor, and Independent Salvagers / Salvager’s Haven.
Wayfinder_Sourcebook.pdf
🚀  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 129 / 136

Starting-campaign structure: The opening shipwreck origin system and rescue-
circumstance framework are already deﬁned. Wayfinder_Sourcebook.pdf
High-level campaign support logic: The DM-facing material already
establishes how captain powers should alter routes, reputation, territory, politics,
and ﬂeet conﬂict without replacing the adventure.
B. Missing worldbuilding
These areas are either absent or only named at a very high level:
Continental geography: The setting is an archipelago world, so continent-scale
geography needs to be replaced with an explicit island-chain / sea-basin structure.
The manuscript does not yet provide that structure. Wayfinder_Sourcebook.pdf
Regional geography: There are named ports and faction hubs, but no region-by-

# Chapter 15: Glossary and Master Index

## Glossary of Terms

- **Captain's Technique:** A magical or extraordinary ability granted by the Wayfinder class, representing the captain's mastery over their vessel, crew, and the elements.
- **Living Ship:** The Wayfinder's magical vessel, progressing in power alongside the captain. Upgrades via Tiers (Driftwood, Captain's, Legendary, Mythic, Path-Maker).
- **Navigator Spirit:** A spectral entity or manifestation of the sea's will that guides the Wayfinder.
- **Shattered Expanse:** The primary setting for the campaign, an ocean dotted with ruins, impossible geography, and shifting magical tides.
- **Tier:** The power scaling system for Captain's Techniques and Vessels. The four Technique Tiers are Least, Lesser, Greater, and Legendary.

# Chapter 18: The World Atlas

*“The world did not end. It merely shattered. What was once a continent of empires is now an ocean of possibilities.”* — First Captain Alaric Thorne

The **Shattered Expanse** is the defining geographical reality of the setting. Roughly 800 years ago, a cataclysm known as the **Sundering Tide** obliterated the ancient supercontinent, fragmenting it into tens of thousands of islands scattered across an endless, turbulent ocean.

## Charted Waters vs. The Unwritten Sea

The ocean is roughly divided into two distinct logistical and magical zones.

### The Charted Waters (35% of the Expanse)
The Charted Waters represent the "civilized" or at least "survivable" regions of the world. These are the major trade routes, the secured archipelagoes, and the naval territories claimed by the dominant factions. 
- **Navigational Reality:** Standard compasses work here. The weather behaves according to predictable patterns, and the ley lines are stable enough to allow for reliable arcane travel.
- **The Great Magical Trade Network:** This is where the bulk of the Pirate Renaissance takes place. Armadas clash over taxation routes, and corsairs raid the fat merchant galleons of the empires.

### The Unwritten Sea (65% of the Expanse)
Beyond the edges of the faction maps lies The Unwritten Sea. This is the untamed, shifting, and wildly magical frontier. 
- **Navigational Reality:** Non-magical compasses spin wildly. Islands move with the tides, and the stars occasionally change position. Navigating the Unwritten Sea requires a Wayfinder, a Navigator Spirit, or suicidal luck.
- **The Discovery Loop:** This is the realm of ancient First Captain ruins, slumbering leviathans, and forgotten magic. Expeditions venture here seeking power, often returning with either legendary wealth or not at all.

## Major Magical Phenomena

The Sundering Tide did not just break the land; it broke the weave of magic itself, creating permanent geographical hazards.

### The Aether Streams
Massive, invisible rivers of magical energy that flow beneath the ocean's surface. Ships equipped with Arcane engines (such as a Tier 3 Legendary Vessel) can lock onto an Aether Stream, allowing them to sail at impossible speeds against the wind. However, these streams occasionally surge, threatening to tear apart hulls that aren't structurally reinforced.

### The Doldrums of Silence
Zones where the wind simply ceases to exist, and all sound is magically dampened. These regions can stretch for hundreds of miles. Ships caught in the Doldrums without magical propulsion or rowers will slowly starve to death. It is rumored that ghosts of the First Fleet haunt the deepest centers of the Silence.

### The Glass Storms
A localized weather event found near psionic ruins. Instead of rain, the clouds precipitate crystalline shards driven by gale-force winds. A Glass Storm can shred a mundane sail in minutes and severely damage a wooden hull. Navigators must either magically shield the ship or outrun the storm front.

## The Vertical Map: The Underdark Ocean
The Shattered Expanse is not limited to the surface. Beneath the waves lies the **Sunless Sea** (the Underdark Ocean). 
Accessible only through massive, whirlpool-like abysses known as "The Drown-Gates," the Sunless Sea is a subterranean ocean lit by bioluminescent fungi and magma vents. It is home to deep-dwelling empires, lost aberrations, and the most dangerous corsairs who use the subterranean waters to bypass surface blockades.

> [!NOTE]
> **DM Guidance on Geography:** The Shattered Expanse is designed to support the core campaign loop: Rumor -> Expedition -> Discovery -> Route -> Trade -> Conflict. By placing high-value ancient ruins in the dangerous Unwritten Sea, and the markets to sell those goods in the Charted Waters, you naturally force the players into conflict with both the environment and the rival factions!


---

# Chapter 19: Regional Gazetteer - The Crimson Dominion

*“Magic is not a gift. It is a weapon. The ocean is not a frontier. It is a territory. Those who disagree will row our galleys until they understand.”* — High Admiral Vane

The Crimson Dominion is the most formidable, expansionist, and terrifying military power operating in the Charted Waters. They are a magocracy built upon a foundation of absolute subjugation, believing that those who wield arcane power possess the divine right to rule, and those who lack it exist solely to serve. 

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Dominion lays claim to the **Cinder Atolls**, a highly volcanic, resource-rich cluster of islands on the eastern edge of the Charted Waters. 

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~2.5 Million
- The Arcane Admiralty & Elite: 5%
- The Iron Legion (Military) & Citizens: 35%
- The Bound (Enslaved Caste): 60%

### The Crimson Citadel (Capital Hub)
The capital is not merely a city; it is a fortress carved directly into the caldera of a dormant, massive volcano. The caldera is open to the sea, providing an impenetrable natural harbor.
- **The Obsidian Spire:** The central tower rising from the harbor water, housing the Arcane Admiralty. It glows with eternal, magical fire.
- **The Ash Markets:** The civilian and merchant districts, built in tiers along the interior walls of the volcano.
- **The Deep Pens:** The horrific slave quarters located near the magma vents, used for geothermal heating and psychological breaking.

### The Iron Shipyards
Located on a neighboring island stripped bare of all vegetation. Here, massive drydocks use bound fire and earth elementals to smelt steel and shape hull plating. The air is choked with smog, and the harbor is constantly churning with the launch of new warships.

### The Border Forts (The Crimson Line)
A perimeter of heavily fortified, magically warded island outposts. Any ship that crosses The Crimson Line without a Dominion writ is immediately fired upon by heavy arcane siege engines.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Dominion’s approach to magic is entirely utilitarian and weaponized.

- **Arcane Magic (The Backbone):** Evocation and Abjuration are the highest-honored schools. Wizards are drafted into the military at a young age. Dominion ships use wands of *Fireball* mounted on gimbals instead of black powder cannons, and project massive *Wall of Force* shields to deflect incoming fire.
- **Psionics (Internal Control):** Psions form the Dominion Inquisition. They are used for long-range, secure fleet communication (bypassing the need for flags or couriers) and for thought-policing the slave populations and lower military ranks. 
- **Spirit Magic (Banned/Exploited):** The Dominion considers Spirit Magic primitive and dangerous. When they encounter elementals or nature spirits, they use Arcane binding circles to trap them, turning them into living batteries to power ship engines and forges.
- **Deep Magic (Feared & Ignored):** The Dominion's arrogance blinds them to the deep ocean. They view the Underdark Ocean as a myth or a wasteland, making their heavily armored surface fleets highly vulnerable to attacks from below.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Dominion operates on a policy of eventual total conquest. 

- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Active Extermination.* The Dominion views pirates as vermin disrupting their divine right to rule the ocean. They hang captured Wayfinders from their rigging as warnings.
- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Cold War / Exploitative Trade.* The Dominion relies on the Consortium for rare spell components and raw materials. They tolerate them—for now—but actively plant spies in Port Meridian, planning for the day they simply annex the trade network.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Shadow War.* A secret, brutal conflict rages. The Dominion loses ships to "mysterious storms" that are actually Drow submarine ambushes. The Admiralty officially denies the Drow exist to avoid looking weak.
- **Spirit Keepers:** *Hostile Exploitation.* The Dominion actively hunts the ancient leviathans the Spirit Keepers protect, seeking to harvest their blood and bone for magical experimentation.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Regulated Tolerance.* The Dominion desperately wants First Captain technology. They allow the Salvagers to operate in their waters, provided the Dominion gets the right of first refusal on all magical artifacts pulled from the Unwritten Sea.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Caste System)

Social mobility in the Dominion is impossible without magical aptitude.

1. **The Arcane Admiralty:** The absolute rulers. Consists of twelve High Mages who hold the rank of Admiral. They dictate law, military strategy, and magical research.
2. **The Magi-Officers:** Commissioned ship captains, tacticians, and court wizards. To hold rank on a ship, one must be capable of casting at least 3rd-level spells.
3. **The Iron Legion:** The non-magical standing army. Highly disciplined, heavily armored marines and city guards. They are respected, but ultimately viewed as expendable shields for the Magi.
4. **The Merchant Class:** State-approved civilians. Bureaucrats, quartermasters, and sanctioned traders who manage the empire's logistics.
5. **The Bound:** The slave caste. Comprised of captured pirates, conquered islanders, and political dissidents. They handle all physical labor, from mining to rowing the massive galleys when the wind fails.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Dominion projects absolute unity, but beneath the surface, it is a nest of vipers.

- **The Traditionalists:** High mages who believe the empire should rely strictly on pure Arcane superiority and look down on those who meddle with foreign magic.
- **The Artificers Guild:** Radicals within the shipyards who want to integrate salvaged, unstable First Captain technology with modern ship designs, often resulting in catastrophic explosions.
- **The Inquisition:** The terrifying psionic secret police. They are supposed to hunt traitors, but they have grown so powerful that even Magi-Officers fear them.
- **The Liberation Front:** A highly secretive, violently suppressed abolitionist movement operating in the deep mines and ship bilges. They communicate via smuggled thieves' cant and are desperate for outside help (a perfect hook for a Wayfinder crew).

## 6. The Military Machine

The Crimson Armada is the most feared sight on the Charted Waters.

- **Ship Design:** Dominion ships are built for intimidation. They are angular, heavily armored with dark iron plating, and painted a stark, blood crimson. They do not rely solely on sails; their massive flagships are propelled by bound fire and water elementals churning paddlewheels.
- **Shipboard Weaponry:** Instead of cannons, they use Arcane Ballistae that fire enchanted bolts, and batteries of battle-mages trained to volley *Lightning Bolt* and *Fireball* in synchronized strikes.
- **Boarding Tactics:** The Iron Legion does not swing over on ropes. Dominion ships use magical grappling hooks that forcefully drag enemy ships against their armored hulls, while Magi cast *Hold Person* and *Web* to paralyze the enemy crew before the heavily armored marines march across the gangplanks.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **High Admiral Vane:** The ruthless, calculating leader of the Arcane Admiralty. A master Evoker who lost an arm in a pirate raid and replaced it with a prosthetic forged of living magma. He seeks to completely eradicate the Free Captains.
- **Inquisitor Kaelen:** The terrifying head of the Dominion's psionic secret police. Kaelen is rarely seen, preferring to project his consciousness into the minds of his agents. He is currently obsessed with rooting out the Liberation Front.
- **Arbiter Zyla:** A highly decorated Magi-Officer who secretly sympathizes with the abolitionists. She commands the frigate *Iron Resolution* and occasionally turns a blind eye to smuggler vessels carrying escaped slaves.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Soldier, Sage, Acolyte, Noble, Prisoner (The Bound).
- **Favored Classes:** Wizard (Evocation, Abjuration), Fighter (Eldritch Knight, Battle Master), Artificer (Artillerist). 
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Dominion PC might be a loyalist trying to prove their worth, an exiled mage who grew a conscience, or a former slave (The Bound) seeking bloody vengeance against their former masters.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Arcane Ballistae:** Siege weapons that don't use ammunition. They draw on the operator's spell slots (or a bound elemental core) to fire bolts of raw force damage.
- **Crimson Iron:** A dense, magically treated steel that grants Resistance to Fire damage. It is incredibly heavy, requiring specialized ship designs to stay buoyant.
- **Wands of the Line:** Standard-issue evocation wands given to Magi-Officers, which can be slotted into shipboard amplification arrays to cast extended-range *Fireballs* during naval combat.


---

# Chapter 20: Regional Gazetteer - The Grand Merchant Consortium

*“We do not conquer islands, Captain. We purchase them. It is infinitely cheaper than war, and the locals even thank us for it.”* — High Factor Vance of the Gold Scale

The Grand Merchant Consortium is the economic heart of the Shattered Expanse. They are not a traditional nation with borders, but rather a hyper-capitalist coalition of trading companies, banking guilds, and mercenary navies. They believe that gold is the only true magic in the world, and they possess enough of it to buy or bury any threat.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Consortium's power does not lie in a contiguous landmass, but in their absolute control over the Great Magical Trade Network.

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~1.2 Million (Direct employees and citizens)
- The High Factors (Ruling Board): <1%
- Guild Members & Contracted Sailors: 40%
- Indentured Laborers & Debtors: 59%

### Port Meridian (Capital Hub)
Port Meridian is a massive, sprawling city-state built across a central, highly strategic archipelago. It is the wealthiest location in the known world. The city is a dizzying array of toll bridges, towering marble banks, and crowded, neon-lit arcane bazaars. The Consortium doesn't "own" the city in a royal sense—they just own the deeds to every single building and ship inside it.
- **The Golden Spire:** The central banking and administration hub where the Board of Factors meets.
- **The Silk Wards:** Opulent districts for high-ranking merchants and visiting diplomats.
- **The Debt Docks:** Slums where those who have defaulted on Consortium loans are forced to work off their debt in the shipyards.

### The Waystations
The Consortium maintains dozens of heavily fortified, neutral trading posts scattered across the Charted Waters. These serve as safe havens, resupply points, and banking outposts.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Consortium views magic purely as a commodity and a logistical tool.

- **Arcane Magic (Logistics & Profit):** The primary magic used by the Consortium. They employ wizards not as soldiers, but as navigators, weather-controllers, and accountants. Illusion magic is heavily regulated to prevent counterfeiting.
- **Psionics (Contracts & Security):** Psions are highly paid to act as living polygraphs during negotiations and to secure vaults against magical intrusion.
- **Spirit Magic (Commodified):** They don't revere spirits; they commodify them. They harvest rare pearls, coral, and leviathan bones to sell to Arcane researchers at a massive markup.
- **Deep Magic (The Black Market):** The Consortium publicly outlaws Deep Magic, but secretly, wealthy Factors pay exorbitant sums for Drow artifacts pulled from the Sunless Sea.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Consortium’s foreign policy is simple: maximize profit, minimize risk.

- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Profitable Nuisance.* They publicly condemn piracy and execute pirates to maintain a "safe" image, but secretly hire Free Captains as deniable privateers to sabotage rival trade routes.
- **Crimson Dominion:** *Cold War / Essential Client.* The Dominion is the Consortium's biggest customer for raw materials. The Consortium walks a razor's edge, selling them enough to profit, but not enough to let the Dominion become completely self-sufficient and annex Port Meridian.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Black Market Partners.* The Consortium officially denies trading with the Drow, while simultaneously maintaining highly lucrative smuggling routes with them through the Drown-Gates.
- **Spirit Keepers:** *Annoying Obstacles.* The Keepers constantly blockade Consortium whaling and logging operations. The Consortium views them as backward zealots holding up progress.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Primary Investors.* The Consortium funds Salvager expeditions into the Unwritten Sea, retaining exclusive rights to auction off any First Captain technology they find.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Corporate Ladder)

Status in the Consortium is entirely determined by wealth and guild rank.

1. **The Board of Factors:** The CEOs of the massive trading companies. They dictate economic policy, interest rates, and naval deployment.
2. **The Guildmasters:** Heads of specific industries (e.g., The Spice Cartel, The Arcane Materials Guild). 
3. **The Gold Cloaks:** The elite, highly paid mercenary army and private navy that enforces Consortium contracts and protects the trade routes.
4. **The Clerks & Sailors:** The middle class. Bureaucrats, accountants, and contracted sailors who keep the gears of commerce turning.
5. **The Indentured:** Those who fell into debt. They are not technically "slaves" like in the Dominion; they are "contractually obligated laborers." The debt is often mathematically impossible to pay off.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Consortium is a cutthroat meritocracy where hostile takeovers are literal.

- **The Expansionists:** Factors who believe the Consortium should build a massive military to actively conquer the Unwritten Sea and monopolize First Captain ruins.
- **The Shadow Brokers:** Guilds deeply invested in the black market, wanting to formalize trade with the Drow and the pirate lords.
- **The Debtors' Union:** A growing, violent underground movement of indentured laborers plotting to burn the central bank ledgers to free millions from generational debt.
- **Corporate Sabotage:** Factors routinely hire assassins, privateers, and spies to ruin their rivals' trade routes and stock prices. 

## 6. The Military Machine

The Consortium's military is entirely privatized. They do not have citizens defending a homeland; they have mercenaries defending cargo.

- **Naval Fleet Composition:** 
  - *Treasure Galleons:* Massive, heavily warded floating vaults designed to transport fortunes. They are slow but practically unsinkable.
  - *The Gold Cloak Escorts:* Sleek, fast, heavily armed frigates that guard the trade lanes.
- **Shipboard Weaponry:** They prioritize defense. Their ships are equipped with advanced Abjuration shielding, enchanted sails for speed, and magical flares to summon nearby mercenary fleets.
- **Combat Tactics:** They prefer to buy off enemies. If forced into a fight, they rely on overwhelming, expensive firepower (e.g., mercenaries armed with wands and enchanted ammunition) and rapid tactical retreats to protect their investments.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **High Factor Vance:** Head of the Gold Scale (the central banking guild). A meticulously dressed, incredibly wealthy human who treats geopolitics like a ledger. He is currently funding Salvager expeditions to secure First Captain weapons.
- **Guildmaster Seraphina:** Leader of the Spice Cartel. She secretly employs a massive network of Free Captain privateers to sabotage her rivals' shipments, all while maintaining plausible deniability.
- **Captain "Ironclad" Thorne:** Commander of the *Golden Defender*, the flagship of the Gold Cloaks. A highly paid mercenary who cares only for his contract, completely devoid of personal morality.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Guild Artisan, Charlatan, Criminal/Spy, Sailor, Noble.
- **Favored Classes:** Rogue (Swashbuckler, Mastermind), Bard (College of Eloquence), Warlock (The Fathomless - flavored as a contract with a sea entity), Wizard (Divination, Illusion).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Consortium PC might be a mid-level manager trying to climb the corporate ladder, a debt-dodger on the run from the Gold Cloaks, or a corporate spy embedded in another faction.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Abjuration Shield Emitters:** Expensive ship modifications that project a temporary, regenerative forcefield around the hull, absorbing a set amount of damage before shattering.
- **Contract Marks:** Magical tattoos given to indentured laborers or sworn mercenaries. They act as tracking devices and can inflict pain if the wearer breaches their contract.
- **Golden Fire:** A highly volatile, alchemically modified Greek Fire that burns even underwater. The Consortium sells it at a premium.


---

# Chapter 21: Regional Gazetteer - The Drowned Silk Houses

*“The surface-dwellers fight over scraps of driftwood and claim they rule the ocean. They do not realize the true power lies in the crushing dark, where their wooden toys splinter and their kings drown.”* — Matron Viconia of House Xorlarrin

The Drowned Silk Houses are not a surface nation; they are an ancient, predatory civilization operating primarily within the Sunless Sea (the Underdark Ocean). They are a fractured society of Drow noble houses who survived a cataclysmic flood centuries ago by adapting their magic to the deep ocean. They view the surface world as a hunting ground, a political chessboard, and a resource pool to be exploited from the shadows.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Silk Houses exist in a perpetual state of twilight, occupying massive caverns and bioluminescent trenches beneath the ocean floor. 

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~800,000 (Drow and deep-sea races)
- The Matron Mothers & Nobles: 2%
- The Crimson Guard & Arcane Scholars: 28%
- The Thralls (Enslaved surface races & lesser aquatic beings): 70%

### Nymorath Deep Harbor (Capital Hub)
Nymorath is a breathtaking and terrifying city built into the walls of a massive underwater trench, illuminated by bioluminescent fungi and chained deep-sea leviathans. A massive sphere of magically maintained air encompasses the noble districts, while the lower slums and slave pens are partially flooded, forcing thralls to rely on water-breathing enchantments to survive. 
- **The Obsidian Web:** The central political arena where the Matron Mothers hold court, negotiate alliances, and plot assassinations.
- **The Abyssal Forges:** Where Drow artificers craft weapons from black coral and deep-sea metals that shatter surface steel.
- **The Drown-Gates:** Massive, magical whirlpool-portals that allow their fleets to instantly surface in the Charted Waters for raids.

### The Trench Outposts
Scattered listening posts and forward operating bases located near major surface trade routes, used to monitor the Merchant Consortium and Crimson Dominion.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Drow possess the most advanced, ancient, and dangerous understanding of magic in the Shattered Expanse.

- **Deep Magic (The Foundation):** The Drow are the undisputed masters of Deep Magic. They use it to manipulate gravity, control water pressure, and mutate deep-sea beasts into war mounts. It is inherently tied to darkness and cold.
- **Arcane Magic (Weaponized):** Highly developed, but focused on stealth, illusion, and assassination. Necromancy is widely practiced, utilizing drowned sailors as cheap, mindless labor.
- **Psionics (Espionage):** Used extensively by their elite spies to mind-read captured captains and communicate silently during surface raids. 
- **Spirit Magic (Corrupted):** They capture ocean spirits and systematically torture and corrupt them, twisting them into dark entities to power their submarine fleets.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Drow do not seek open war; they seek absolute, unseen control.

- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Lucrative Exploitation.* The Drow officially do not exist according to the Consortium, but in reality, the two factions run massive, secret black-market smuggling rings. The Drow sell ancient artifacts and poisons for surface luxuries and slaves.
- **Crimson Dominion:** *The Shadow War.* The Dominion is the only surface power that poses a military threat. The Drow actively sabotage Dominion shipyards, sink their fleets in "unexplained storms," and assassinate their mages to keep them fractured.
- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Convenient Pawns.* The Drow frequently manipulate pirate fleets into attacking Dominion or Consortium targets by leaking false maps or rumors, using them as unwitting mercenaries.
- **Spirit Keepers:** *Hated Enemies.* The Keepers actively try to heal the ocean spirits the Drow corrupt. The Drow view the Keepers as naive fools and will slaughter them on sight.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Prey.* The Drow consider all First Captain artifacts in the Unwritten Sea to be their rightful property. They actively hunt Salvager vessels that delve too deep.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Matriarchy)

Drow society is a brutal, paranoid matriarchy where advancement is achieved through betrayal and assassination.

1. **The Matron Mothers:** The absolute rulers of the Silk Houses. High Priestesses of Deep Magic who command unquestioned obedience.
2. **The Noble Houses:** The extended families of the Matrons. They constantly war with one another in secret for political favor.
3. **The Crimson Guard:** Elite, fanatically loyal male warriors and assassins who serve the Matrons.
4. **The Male Arcane Scholars:** While denied true political power, male wizards are highly valued for their magical research and tactical knowledge.
5. **The Thralls:** The enslaved underclass. Comprised of captured surface sailors, tritons, and Kuo-toa. They are considered disposable and are often used as biological experiments.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Silk Houses are united only in their hatred of the surface; internally, they are a snake pit.

- **House Xorlarrin vs. House Baenre:** A centuries-old blood feud between the two most powerful houses. Xorlarrin favors aggressive surface raids, while Baenre prefers economic manipulation and smuggling.
- **The Bregan D'aerthe (The Mercenaries):** A rogue faction of exiled male nobles and elite assassins who operate independently, selling their services to the highest bidder (even surface dwellers).
- **The Cult of the First Captains:** A heretical, underground religious movement that believes the First Captains were actually ancient Drow gods, and seeks to awaken the slumbering horrors of the Unwritten Sea to wipe out the surface completely.

## 6. The Military Machine

The Drow do not fight fair. They strike from the shadows, cripple their enemies, and vanish before a retaliation can be organized.

- **Naval Fleet Composition:** They do not use wooden sailing ships. They utilize biological submarines—massive, hollowed-out, undead sea leviathans (like giant armored squids or skeletal whales) piloted by Necromancers. 
- **Combat Tactics:** They attack from below. Their "ships" breach the surface, deploying elite boarding parties that use innate darkness magic to blind the enemy crew. 
- **Shipboard Weaponry:** They do not use cannons. They use sonic projectors that can shatter wooden hulls, and magical torpedoes that rapidly freeze the water around an enemy ship, trapping it in ice.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **Matron Viconia:** The ancient, terrifying head of House Xorlarrin. She is a master of Deep Magic and seeks to completely collapse the surface world's trade network by manipulating the Free Captains into war with the Dominion.
- **Jarlaxle Baenre:** The charismatic, flamboyant leader of Bregan D'aerthe. He operates on the surface more than any other Drow, dealing in secrets, assassinations, and rare magical artifacts. He views the surface world as highly entertaining.
- **Vhaeraun's Blade (Zin):** A heavily scarred, silent male assassin of the Crimson Guard who has begun questioning the absolute rule of the Matron Mothers after encountering a surface-dwelling Cleric of Eilistraee.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Outlander, Urchin, Spy, Faction Agent, Prisoner (Thrall).
- **Favored Classes:** Rogue (Assassin, Arcane Trickster), Ranger (Gloom Stalker), Sorcerer (Aberrant Mind, Shadow), Cleric (Trickery).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Drow PC is almost certainly an exile, a runaway Thrall, or a Bregan D'aerthe mercenary acting as a "consultant" for a Wayfinder crew. Trust will be a major, ongoing roleplay element.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Sonic Projectors:** Cannons that fire concentrated blasts of sound instead of iron balls. Highly effective at shattering wood and deafening surface crews.
- **Piwafwi (Cloak of Elvenkind):** Drow cloaks woven from spider silk and infused with shadow magic, granting superior stealth. They degrade rapidly if exposed to direct sunlight for extended periods.
- **Deep-Breather Gills:** Biological symbiotes (often looking like a slimy collar) forced upon Thralls that allow them to breathe underwater but cause intense pain if they remain out of the water for too long.


---

# Chapter 22: Regional Gazetteer - The Free Captains Alliance

*“The Dominion claims the ocean by divine right. The Consortium claims it by contract. We claim it because our sails are full, our guns are loaded, and the horizon belongs to whoever is bold enough to chase it.”* — Pirate King "Hurricane" Jax

The Free Captains Alliance is not a government; it is a chaotic, rowdy, and fiercely independent confederacy of pirate lords, privateers, explorers, and exiles. They are bound together not by laws, but by a shared hatred of empire and a mutual agreement known as "The Corsair's Code." To the rest of the world, they are lawless terrorists. To themselves, they are the only truly free souls on the Shattered Expanse.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Alliance operates out of the **Jagged Reefs**, a vast, treacherous maze of razor-sharp coral and shifting sandbars that only the most skilled (or magically guided) navigators can traverse without tearing their hulls open.

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~400,000 (Highly transient)
- The Pirate Lords (The Brethren Court): <1%
- Wayfinders, Privateers, & Sworn Crews: 60%
- Smugglers, Tavern-Keepers, & Fence Merchants: 39%

### Blackwake Harbor (Capital Hub)
Blackwake Harbor is a massive, floating city that shouldn't exist. It is constructed entirely from lashed-together galleons, ancient driftwood, and salvaged hulls suspended within a calm lagoon in the center of the Jagged Reefs. It is a city of rope bridges, swinging lanterns, and perpetual celebration (or brawling).
- **The Brethren's Table:** A massive, hollowed-out hull of a first-rate ship-of-the-line where the Pirate Lords meet to vote on the rare occasions the Alliance needs to act as a unified fleet.
- **The Powder Keg:** The chaotic tavern district, where fortunes are won and lost on games of dice, and where most crews are recruited.
- **The Chop Shop:** A massive drydock dedicated to refitting stolen ships, scrubbing their identifying marks, and outfitting them with illegal weaponry.

### The Hidden Coves
The Alliance maintains dozens of secret inlets, hidden coves, and disguised anchorages throughout the Charted Waters, allowing them to strike and vanish before the Dominion or Consortium can respond.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Free Captains are highly pragmatic about magic; if it keeps the ship afloat or helps them steal gold, they use it.

- **Spirit Magic (The Lifeline):** The most common and respected magic among the Captains. Many crews employ shamans to speak with the winds, beg safe passage from minor ocean spirits, and bond with Navigator Spirits to guide them through the Reefs.
- **Arcane Magic (Stolen & Scavenged):** They rarely have formally trained wizards. Instead, they rely on rogue sorcerers, stolen wands, and scavenged scrolls. Their magic is chaotic, loud, and unpredictable.
- **Deep Magic (Taboo):** Most Captains are highly superstitious and consider the deep ocean cursed. Those who practice Deep Magic are viewed with intense suspicion and are often accused of making deals with the Drow.
- **Psionics (Untrusted):** Psions are rarely found on pirate ships. The concept of someone reading thoughts violates the core tenet of piratical freedom; mind-readers are usually thrown overboard.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Alliance views the world through a lens of targets, threats, and temporary allies.

- **Crimson Dominion:** *Kill on Sight.* The Dominion represents everything the Captains hate: slavery, conformity, and imperial control. The Alliance will actively target Dominion slaver ships and border forts.
- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Primary Targets (But Occasional Employers).* Consortium treasure galleons are the ultimate prize. However, the Captains are pragmatic; they will gladly accept a Consortium "Letter of Marque" to act as privateers against the Dominion, provided the gold is good.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Superstitious Terror.* The Free Captains rarely engage the Drow willingly. When ships go missing in calm waters, the pirates blame the Drow. 
- **Spirit Keepers:** *Wary Respect.* The Keepers are one of the few factions the Pirates try not to anger. A pirate captain knows better than to hunt a leviathan in Keeper territory, lest the ocean itself turn against them.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Friendly Rivals.* The Captains frequently trade with the Salvagers. They often race each other into the Unwritten Sea to claim First Captain wrecks.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Meritocracy)

In Blackwake Harbor, nobility means nothing. You are only worth the strength of your sword arm, the loyalty of your crew, and the size of your ship.

1. **The Pirate Lords:** The most feared and successful captains in the Expanse. They command massive fleets (armadas) and hold a seat on the Brethren Court.
2. **The Wayfinders / Captains:** Independent ship commanders. Within the Alliance, every Captain is considered a sovereign ruler of their own deck.
3. **The Quartermasters:** Highly respected officers elected by the crew to represent their interests, distribute loot, and ensure the Captain doesn't abuse their power.
4. **The Sworn Crew:** The sailors who have sworn the Corsair's Code. They are guaranteed a share of the plunder and the right to vote on the ship's destination.
5. **The Scallywags (Landlubbers):** The un-sworn. Merchants, tavern keeps, and dockworkers who live in Blackwake Harbor but do not sail.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Alliance is constantly on the verge of tearing itself apart, held together only by external threats.

- **The Old Guard:** Traditional pirate lords who believe the Alliance should focus purely on raiding the Consortium and enjoying their freedom.
- **The Abolitionists:** Radical captains who believe the Alliance's primary goal should be a full-scale war to destroy the Crimson Dominion and free the slave caste.
- **The Traitor Captains:** Crews who secretly accept pardons and paychecks from the Dominion to hunt down other pirates (the ultimate sin in Blackwake).
- **The Mutiny Problem:** Every Captain is one bad voyage away from being overthrown by their own crew. Democracy on a pirate ship is a double-edged sword.

## 6. The Military Machine

The Free Captains do not have a uniform standing navy. They fight a guerrilla war at sea.

- **Naval Fleet Composition:** A chaotic mix of everything. Stolen Dominion frigates, retrofitted Consortium merchantmen, and custom-built, impossibly fast blockade runners. No two ships look alike.
- **Combat Tactics:** Speed and surprise. They rely on ambush tactics, utilizing thick fog (often magically summoned), narrow straits, and boarding actions. They prefer to capture ships intact rather than sink them.
- **Shipboard Weaponry:** A mix of traditional black-powder cannons (which they favor for their reliability), scavenged Arcane Ballistae, and whatever experimental, highly volatile First Captain weaponry they managed to salvage.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **Pirate King "Hurricane" Jax:** The boisterous, dangerously unpredictable leader of the Brethren Court. A Storm Sorcerer who claims to have won his crown in a drinking contest with a kraken. 
- **Captain Elara "Ghost-Wake" Vance:** A brilliant tactician and defector from the Crimson Dominion's Magi-Officers. She commands the *Reckoning*, a heavily modified frigate dedicated solely to hunting Dominion slaver ships.
- **Quartermaster Thatch:** The pragmatic, ruthless manager of Blackwake's "Chop Shop." If you need a stolen ship's identity scrubbed or a cursed artifact fenced, Thatch is the man to see.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Sailor, Pirate, Criminal, Folk Hero, Entertainer.
- **Favored Classes:** Fighter (Battle Master, Champion), Rogue (Swashbuckler, Thief), Barbarian (Storm Herald, Berserker), Sorcerer (Storm Sorcery).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Free Captain PC might be a newly minted Wayfinder looking to make a name for themselves, a runaway seeking true freedom, or an exiled noble seeking revenge against the faction that wronged them.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Black-Powder Firearms:** The Alliance is the primary manufacturer and user of flintlock pistols and muskets. While less reliable than a wand, they don't require magical aptitude to blow a hole in a Dominion marine.
- **Scavenger Sails:** Canvas sails patched with the hides of minor elementals or sea monsters, allowing a ship to catch magical Aether winds that normal canvas would tear in.
- **The Corsair's Coin:** A magically minted coin given to Sworn Crew. It acts as a token of safe passage in Blackwake Harbor and can cast *Sending* once to contact the ship's Quartermaster in an emergency.


---

# Chapter 23: Regional Gazetteer - The Spirit Keepers

*“The ocean is not a road for your merchants, nor a battlefield for your empires. It is a living, breathing entity, and you are sailing across its open wounds. We are the immune system.”* — High Elder Naia of the Kelp Wardens

The Spirit Keepers are a reclusive, deeply spiritual, and fiercely territorial faction dedicated to preserving the ancient magic of the ocean. They believe that the Shattered Expanse is fundamentally broken, kept alive only by the slumbering leviathans and the chaotic elemental spirits that inhabit the Aether Streams. To the Consortium, they are ecoterrorists. To the Dominion, they are primitive heretics. To the Keepers themselves, they are the only thing preventing the world from drowning.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Spirit Keepers do not build cities of stone or iron. They reside within the **Verdant Expanse**, a massive, magically accelerated kelp forest that stretches for hundreds of miles, thick enough to entangle and sink heavy galleons. 

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~250,000
- The Council of Elders: <1%
- The Wardens & Shamans: 30%
- The Tenders (Civilians & Gatherers): 69%

### The Last Harbor (Capital Hub)
The Last Harbor is a hidden sanctuary located deep within the Verdant Expanse. It is woven directly into the canopy of ancient, petrified mangrove trees that rise hundreds of feet out of the water. The harbor is not visible from the open ocean; a ship must be guided by a bonded Navigator Spirit to find the entrance through the shifting kelp walls.
- **The Heartwood Spire:** A massive, hollowed-out ancient tree where the Council of Elders communes directly with ancient ocean spirits.
- **The Leviathan Nurseries:** Protected lagoons where the Keepers tend to wounded sea beasts and foster the growth of young elementals.
- **The Wreckage Gardens:** Instead of a traditional shipyard, the Keepers use magic to accelerate coral and kelp growth over sunken ships, creating living, organic vessels.

### The Aether Shrines
Small, heavily warded outposts located along the major magical currents (Aether Streams). The Keepers use these to monitor the flow of magic and detect arcane pollution from the Dominion or Consortium.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Keepers' entire society is built around a symbiotic relationship with magic, rather than exploitation.

- **Spirit Magic (The Core):** The foundation of their culture. Every Keeper ship is bonded with a minor elemental or spirit. Their shamans can summon localized storms, command the tides, and communicate over vast distances through the water itself.
- **Divine Magic (Revered):** They have a high concentration of Clerics and Druids dedicated to nature deities or the ocean itself. Healing and preservation are highly valued.
- **Arcane Magic (Distrusted):** They view Arcane magic—specifically Evocation and Necromancy—as a brutal, unnatural forcing of the world's laws. They consider Dominion wizards to be dangerous vandals.
- **Deep Magic (The Enemy):** They recognize Deep Magic as a corrupting, horrific force. They know what the Drow do to ocean spirits, and they consider the eradication of Deep Magic to be a holy crusade.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Keepers are inherently defensive, but they will strike with overwhelming, terrifying force if their sanctuaries are threatened.

- **Crimson Dominion:** *Holy War.* The Dominion hunts leviathans for components and pollutes the Aether Streams with arcane engines. The Keepers actively sink Dominion logging and whaling fleets.
- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Hostile Blockade.* The Keepers despise the Consortium's rampant industrialization and overfishing. They frequently blockade Consortium trade routes, using summoned storms to force merchant galleons to turn back or sink.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Mortal Enemies.* The Drow corrupt the ocean spirits that the Keepers revere. The Keepers have specialized strike teams (The Deep-Divers) trained specifically to hunt Drow submarines and cleanse corrupted waters.
- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Wary Neutrality.* The pirates respect the power of the ocean and usually leave the Verdant Expanse alone. In return, the Keepers occasionally offer sanctuary to pirate crews fleeing the Dominion, provided they swear an oath to harm no spirits.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Suspicious Watchers.* The Keepers allow the Salvagers to explore, but they carefully monitor what artifacts are pulled from the Unwritten Sea, intervening if they believe a relic is too dangerous for humanity to possess.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Symbiocracy)

Status among the Keepers is not determined by wealth or bloodline, but by one's connection to the spirits.

1. **The Council of Elders:** The oldest and most spiritually attuned shamans and druids. They do not give orders; they interpret the will of the ocean.
2. **The Wardens:** The elite warriors and captains. They are physically bonded to minor elementals, granting them supernatural speed and strength in the water.
3. **The Shamans / Navigators:** The spellcasters who maintain the Aether Shrines and guide the organic ships through the ocean.
4. **The Tenders:** The civilian population who cultivate the kelp forests, craft organic weapons, and raise the young. 
5. **The Penitent:** Outsiders (often ex-pirates or defectors from the Dominion) who are seeking redemption and are serving a period of grueling, humbling labor to prove their respect for the ocean.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Keepers are generally united in purpose, but deeply divided on methodology.

- **The Preservers (The Majority):** Believe the Keepers should remain isolationist, defending the Verdant Expanse and healing the ocean slowly.
- **The Cleansing Tide:** A radical, militant sect that believes the only way to save the ocean is to actively wage war on the surface factions and completely dismantle the Dominion and Consortium.
- **The Firstborn Cult:** A secretive group that believes the First Captains were not heroes, but the original corrupters of the ocean, and that all First Captain artifacts must be destroyed, putting them in direct conflict with the Salvagers.

## 6. The Military Machine

The Keepers do not fight in line-of-battle formations. They use the ocean itself as a weapon.

- **Naval Fleet Composition:** They sail "Living Ships"—salvaged wrecks that have been completely overgrown and fused together by magically hardened coral and iron-wood kelp. They are incredibly resilient and self-repairing.
- **Combat Tactics:** Environmental warfare. They rarely engage in broadside cannon duels. Instead, they summon localized squalls, manipulate currents to smash enemy ships into reefs, and use bonded sea creatures (like giant squids or elementals) to tear hulls apart from beneath.
- **Shipboard Weaponry:** They do not use black powder. They use enchanted ballistae that fire explosive coral shards, and rely on their shamans to cast *Call Lightning* and *Control Water* to devastate enemy crews.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **High Elder Naia:** The ancient, serene voice of the Council of Elders. She is bonded to a massive, ancient sea turtle and advocates for isolationism and healing the ocean, rather than war.
- **Warden Kael:** The aggressive, militant leader of the Cleansing Tide. He believes the only way to protect the Verdant Expanse is to sink every Dominion and Consortium ship that dares cross the horizon.
- **Tender Maris:** A highly skilled crafter of "Living Ships" and a sympathetic contact for Wayfinders. She frequently trades minor magical charms to independent captains in exchange for news of the outside world.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Hermit, Outlander, Sailor, Acolyte, Folk Hero.
- **Favored Classes:** Druid (Circle of the Land - Coast, Circle of the Moon), Cleric (Nature, Tempest), Ranger (Swarmkeeper, Hunter), Monk (Way of the Four Elements).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Keeper PC is likely on a sacred pilgrimage to find the source of the Aether Streams, a Warden hunting a specific Drow that escaped, or a member of the "Penitent" trying to work off a past sin against the ocean.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Living Hull Plating:** Magically grown iron-wood kelp and coral that can be grafted onto a standard ship. It provides temporary hit points to the hull and slowly regenerates over time when submerged in seawater.
- **Breath-Weed:** A highly prized, magically cultivated kelp that, when chewed, grants the user the ability to breathe underwater for 1 hour. The Keepers trade this very rarely.
- **Storm-Callers:** Conches infused with elemental air magic. When blown, they can alter the direction of the wind for a short duration, crucial for escaping a naval ambush.


---

# Chapter 24: Regional Gazetteer - The Independent Salvagers

*“The world ended once already. The evidence is scattered across the ocean floor. The fools in the Dominion think they can ignore it. We know that whoever controls the wreckage of the past, controls the future.”* — Chief Archaeologist Silas Vance

The Independent Salvagers (often referred to simply as "The Guild") are a faction of extreme risk-takers, rogue historians, artificers, and deep-sea divers. They are dedicated to one singular, incredibly dangerous goal: retrieving the lost, highly advanced, and highly unstable technology of the "First Captains" from the Unwritten Sea. They operate on the fringes of society, driven by a mix of scholarly obsession and raw greed.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Salvagers do not hold territory in the traditional sense. They are nomadic, following rumors and shifting Aether currents toward ancient ruins. However, they maintain one permanent, neutral stronghold.

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~150,000 (Highly dispersed)
- The Guild Masters (Lead Archaeologists): 2%
- The Divers & Artificers (Active Crews): 48%
- Support Staff, Appraisers, & Fences: 50%

### Salvager's Haven (Capital Hub)
Salvager's Haven is built entirely inside the skeletal remains of a truly massive, petrified leviathan that washed up on a barren atoll near the edge of the Unwritten Sea. The ribs of the beast arch over the city like a cathedral ceiling, draped in heavy canvas to keep out the harsh sun. It is a city of workshops, black markets, and intense paranoia.
- **The Athenaeum:** A heavily guarded library where recovered First Captain logs, star-charts, and technical schematics are translated and hoarded.
- **The Decompression Wards:** Hospitals specifically designed to treat divers suffering from "The Bends" or magical radiation poisoning from handling unstable artifacts.
- **The Auction Block:** A massive, reinforced amphitheater where the Consortium and the Dominion quietly outbid each other for recovered weaponry.

### The Diving Bells (Mobile Outposts)
Massive, heavily armored floating platforms equipped with heavy cranes and diving bells, acting as temporary forward operating bases above newly discovered underwater ruins.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Salvagers use magic purely as a tool to aid their excavations and survive the crushing depths.

- **Arcane Magic (The Toolkit):** Abjuration is highly valued for shielding against traps and magical radiation. Transmutation is essential for *Water Breathing* and manipulating heavy wreckage.
- **Deep Magic (The Necessary Evil):** They are the only surface faction that actively studies Deep Magic. They don't worship it like the Drow, but they use it to counteract the crushing pressure of the deep ocean. This makes them highly suspicious to other factions.
- **Psionics (Artifact Bonding):** Many First Captain artifacts require a psionic "handshake" to operate. Psions are highly prized and highly paid to act as keys to these ancient machines.
- **Spirit Magic (Ignored):** They view Spirit Magic as superstitious nonsense that gets in the way of scientific discovery.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Salvagers try to remain neutral, playing the major powers against each other to fund their expeditions.

- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Primary Benefactors.* The Consortium funds most Salvager expeditions in exchange for the first right of refusal on any discovered technology. The relationship is highly transactional.
- **Crimson Dominion:** *Tense Tolerance.* The Dominion wants the artifacts but hates paying for them. They frequently try to bully or annex Salvager fleets, forcing the Salvagers to hire Free Captains as escorts.
- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Friendly Associates.* The pirates provide protection, and the Salvagers fence strange magical loot the pirates don't understand. Many Wayfinders consider themselves freelance Salvagers.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Lethal Competition.* The Drow consider the Unwritten Sea their territory. Encounters between Drow submarines and Salvager diving bells always end in violence.
- **Spirit Keepers:** *The Watchdogs.* The Keepers despise the Salvagers for unearthing things that should remain buried. They will actively sink a Salvager ship if they believe the artifact on board is too dangerous.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Academic Meritocracy)

Rank in the Guild is determined by what you have discovered, and more importantly, what you have survived.

1. **The Guild Masters (Chief Archaeologists):** The few who have successfully commanded major expeditions into the Unwritten Sea and returned with paradigm-shifting technology.
2. **The Artificers / Translators:** The scholars who can actually figure out how to make First Captain technology work without it detonating.
3. **The Master Divers:** The veterans. Anyone who has survived more than ten deep-sea dives into ancient ruins is treated with immense respect (and usually sports several magical prosthetics).
4. **The Dredgers:** The rank-and-file sailors and laborers who operate the cranes, winches, and diving bells.
5. **The Investors:** Wealthy outsiders (usually Consortium) who fund the expeditions but have no operational authority. 

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Guild is fractured by differing philosophies on what to do with the technology they find.

- **The Preservationists:** Believe artifacts should be studied, cataloged, and kept in the Athenaeum for the good of all, rather than sold to warmongers.
- **The Profiteers:** Believe the only purpose of the Guild is to get rich selling weapons to the Dominion and Consortium.
- **The Integrationists:** Radical artificers who actively replace their own body parts with First Captain technology, viewing it as the next step in human evolution.
- **The Curse-Touched:** Divers who have been driven mad by the magical radiation of the deep ruins, who occasionally form violent, unpredictable cults within the lower levels of the Haven.

## 6. The Military Machine

The Salvagers do not have a standing army. They rely on mercenaries for defense and experimental weaponry for survival.

- **Naval Fleet Composition:** They sail heavily modified, slow, incredibly sturdy salvage vessels. These ships look like floating factories, bristling with cranes, winches, and reinforced diving cages. 
- **Combat Tactics:** Flee and cover. They avoid naval battles at all costs. If cornered, they will utilize untested, highly volatile First Captain weaponry, which is just as likely to sink their own ship as the enemy's.
- **Shipboard Weaponry:** Experimental. A Salvager ship might be armed with a cannon that fires bolts of pure gravity, or a shielding device that turns the ship temporarily ethereal, all powered by unstable, glowing crystals.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **Chief Archaeologist Silas Vance:** An aging, paranoid genius who has integrated several First Captain ocular implants into his skull. He firmly believes the First Captains didn't die out, but left the planet entirely.
- **Master Diver "Iron-Lung" Kael:** A legendary diver whose torso is almost entirely encased in a magical, pressure-regulating suit. He is the only man known to have survived a direct encounter with a Drow Leviathan and lived to tell the tale.
- **Appraiser Lira:** The ruthless head of the Auction Block. She speaks seven languages, can identify a fake artifact at fifty paces, and has the Consortium wrapped around her finger.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Sage, Guild Artisan, Sailor, Charlatan, Hermit.
- **Favored Classes:** Artificer (All subclasses, especially Battlesmith and Armorer), Wizard (Transmutation, Scribes), Rogue (Thief, Arcane Trickster), Fighter (Psi Warrior).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Salvager PC is likely an adrenaline junkie, an academic desperate to make a name for themselves, or someone severely in debt to the Consortium looking for one big score in the Unwritten Sea.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Diving Suits (Pressure Armor):** Heavy, magically sealed suits of armor that allow deep-sea exploration, providing immunity to the crushing pressure of the depths but imposing disadvantage on Dexterity checks.
- **Unstable Energy Cores:** Glowing crystals pulled from ruins. They can be used to instantly recharge spell slots or power magic items, but carry a high risk of wild magic surges or explosive failure.
- **The Translator's Monocle:** A highly coveted magic item that casts *Comprehend Languages* specifically calibrated for ancient, dead scripts and First Captain cipher logs.


---

# Chapter 25: Religion & The Cosmic Forces

*“The mainlanders pray to gods of iron and gold. They build cathedrals to shield themselves from the rain. But out here on the open ocean, when the sky turns black and the waves crest higher than your mast, you don’t pray for a contract or a law. You pray to whatever entity is currently deciding whether or not to swallow you whole.”* — Wayfinder Chaplain Silas

Religion in the Shattered Expanse is deeply divided. Unlike other fantasy settings where a unified pantheon watches over the world, the Charted Waters are caught in a theological cold war. On one side are the "New Gods"—deities of civilization, law, commerce, and human ambition, brought by the empires that settled the islands. On the other side are the "Old Entities"—primordial, terrifying forces of nature, chaos, and the deep ocean that were here long before humanity arrived.

---

## 1. The Civilized Pantheon (The "New Gods")

The New Gods are the deities of the mainland, the Crimson Dominion, and the Grand Merchant Consortium. They represent the triumph of civilization over the wild.

### The Iron Arbiter
**God of Law, Conquest, and the Forge**
- **Alignment:** Lawful Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Order, War, Forge
- **Worshippers:** The Crimson Dominion, magistrates, soldiers, armorers.
- **Lore:** Depicted as a massive, faceless knight clad in impenetrable plate armor, holding a perfectly balanced scale in one hand and a heavy maul in the other. He teaches that the world is chaotic and must be brought to heel through discipline, strict hierarchy, and military might. The Dominion views him as their patron deity, using his doctrine to justify their subjugation of the Expanse.
- **Symbol:** A red anvil struck by an iron hammer.

### The Golden Ledger
**Deity of Commerce, Contracts, and Wealth**
- **Alignment:** True Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Knowledge, Trickery
- **Worshippers:** The Grand Merchant Consortium, bankers, smugglers, guildmasters.
- **Lore:** A genderless, multifaceted entity often depicted as a golden figure holding an endlessly unspooling scroll. The Golden Ledger does not care about good or evil, only that debts are paid, contracts are honored, and wealth circulates. The high priests of the Ledger are essentially divine accountants. To break a sworn contract in the Consortium is not just illegal; it is a profound sin against the Ledger.
- **Symbol:** A golden coin with a keyhole in the center.

### The Guiding Star
**Goddess of Navigation, Hope, and Safe Voyages**
- **Alignment:** Neutral Good
- **Suggested Domains:** Light, Twilight, Peace
- **Worshippers:** Wayfinders, honest sailors, navigators, cartographers.
- **Lore:** Depicted as a glowing, ethereal woman holding a celestial astrolabe. She is the most widely worshipped of the New Gods among the common folk of the Expanse. She does not calm the storms—that is the domain of the Old Entities—but she provides the light to navigate through them.
- **Symbol:** A silver, eight-pointed compass rose.

### The Hearth-Keeper
**Goddess of Agriculture, Hearth, and Community**
- **Alignment:** Lawful Good
- **Suggested Domains:** Life, Nature
- **Worshippers:** Island farmers, kelp-harvesters, commoners, healers.
- **Lore:** The islands of the Expanse are harsh, and not everyone is a soldier or a merchant. The Hearth-Keeper is the motherly figure who ensures the kelp farms yield and the coastal storms don't wash away the villages. Her clerics are often local doctors or midwives.
- **Symbol:** A woven basket of glowing kelp.

### The Silent Archivist
**God of Secrets, History, and Espionage**
- **Alignment:** True Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Knowledge, Trickery
- **Worshippers:** Spies, intelligence officers, scholars, the Consortium's "Shadow Brokers".
- **Lore:** A cloaked figure whose face is a blank scroll. The Archivist teaches that knowledge is the ultimate weapon, and that history belongs to those who control the narrative. Temples to the Archivist are hidden libraries where secrets are traded like currency.
- **Symbol:** A closed eye with a keyhole in the pupil.

---

## 2. The Free Gods (Deities of Chaos)

The Free Captains and outcasts of the Expanse often reject organized religion, but they hold deep superstitions and revere certain chaotic forces.

### The Laughing Storm
**God of Revelry, Rebellion, and Luck**
- **Alignment:** Chaotic Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Tempest, Trickery
- **Worshippers:** Free Captains, gamblers, bards, rebels.
- **Lore:** Not so much a god as a personification of pirate philosophy. The Laughing Storm is said to be the spirit of a legendary rogue who stole a divine spark and threw it into the ocean. He rewards the bold, the foolish, and the free. A common pirate blessing is, "May the Storm laugh with you, not at you."
- **Symbol:** A pair of crossed cutlasses over a grinning skull.

### The Drowned Avenger
**Deity of the Sunken Dead, Vengeance, and Mutiny**
- **Alignment:** Chaotic Evil
- **Suggested Domains:** Death, War
- **Worshippers:** Mutineers, the wronged, privateers seeking bloody revenge.
- **Lore:** A grim, relentless entity prayed to by those who have been betrayed. When a sailor is keel-hauled or a slave is tossed overboard, they often use their dying breath to curse their murderers in the Avenger's name. It is said the Avenger commands a ghost fleet of every ship sunk by treachery.
- **Symbol:** A skeletal hand rising from the waves holding a rusted dagger.

---

## 3. The Old Entities (The Primordial Powers)

The Old Entities do not have churches. They do not care about mortal morality. Worshipping them is less about seeking salvation and more about negotiating survival.

### The Slumbering Leviathans
**The Physical Gods of the Ocean**
- **Alignment:** Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Nature, Tempest (Cleric) / The Fathomless (Warlock)
- **Worshippers:** The Spirit Keepers, druids, deep-sea shamans.
- **Lore:** The Keepers do not pray to invisible gods in the sky; they pray to the very real, continent-sized beasts sleeping beneath the crust of the ocean. They believe these Leviathans generate the magical Aether Streams that keep the world alive. When a Leviathan stirs, tsunamis wipe out islands. Their clerics draw power directly from the ambient magic these beasts radiate.
- **Symbol:** A stylized wave crashing over a massive eye.

### The First Captains (The "Cargo Cults")
**The Ancient Precursors**
- **Alignment:** Unknown
- **Suggested Domains:** Knowledge (Cleric) / The Great Old One (Warlock)
- **Worshippers:** The Independent Salvagers, Doomsday Cults, mad artificers.
- **Lore:** The extinct civilization that ruled the world before the flood. Some view them as gods who ascended, while others (like the Salvagers) worship their *technology*. This "Cargo Cult" mentality leads artificers to treat schematics like holy scripture, praying to engines to start and performing maintenance as religious rituals. 
- **Symbol:** An intricate, glowing geometric cipher (often tattooed on the worshipper).

### The Deep Mother
**The Abyssal Horror**
- **Alignment:** Chaotic Evil
- **Suggested Domains:** Death, Grave (Cleric) / The Hexblade (Warlock)
- **Worshippers:** The Drowned Silk Houses (Drow), cultists of the deep.
- **Lore:** The horrific entity that the Drow turned to when the world flooded. The Deep Mother is not a spider, but an oceanic horror of crushing pressure, bioluminescence, and parasitic control. She resides in the darkest trenches of the Sunless Sea, demanding the corruption of surface spirits and absolute matriarchal dominance. 
- **Symbol:** A black, jagged silhouette of a multi-tentacled leviathan clutching a pearl.

---

## 4. Mechanical Integration for Players

When creating a character in the Wayfinder setting, consider how your class connects to these cosmic forces:

### Clerics
- **Order / War / Forge:** You are likely an Inquisitor, War-Priest, or Armorer of the Crimson Dominion, enforcing the Iron Arbiter's will.
- **Tempest / Nature:** You are a shaman of the Spirit Keepers, drawing power from the ocean itself, violently opposed to industrialization.
- **Light / Twilight / Peace:** You are a beacon of hope for a Wayfinder crew, guiding them through the treacherous Jagged Reefs via the Guiding Star.
- **Death / Grave:** You are a rogue Drow priestess of the Deep Mother, or a bitter pirate seeking vengeance through the Drowned Avenger.

### Warlocks
- **The Fathomless:** Your patron is a specific, ancient Leviathan or a powerful, unbound ocean elemental. You are a conduit for the sea's wrath.
- **The Great Old One:** You touched a humming, glowing artifact pulled from the Unwritten Sea, and now the fractured, digital-like consciousness of a First Captain speaks to you in mathematical riddles.
- **The Hexblade:** You wield a weapon forged of black coral and abyssal iron, gifted to you by an exile of the Drowned Silk Houses, or pulled from a sunken wreck.

### Paladins
- **Oath of Conquest:** A perfect fit for the Crimson Dominion's Iron Legion, swearing to bring law to the chaotic seas.
- **Oath of the Open Sea (or Devotion):** A Wayfinder or Free Captain who has sworn to protect the freedom of the Charted Waters against the tyranny of the empires.
- **Oath of the Ancients:** A heavily armored warden of the Spirit Keepers, sworn to protect the Verdant Expanse and the slumbering Leviathans from the taint of Deep Magic.

### Sorcerers
- **Storm Sorcery:** You were born during a magical super-cell, blessed (or cursed) by the Laughing Storm. You are highly prized by the Free Captains.
- **Aberrant Mind:** You were exposed to the deep-sea radiation of the Unwritten Sea, or survived a parasitic encounter with a minion of the Deep Mother.

### Barbarians
- **Path of the Storm Herald:** You are a warrior of the Spirit Keepers, channeling the raw elemental power of the Aether Streams.
- **Path of the Zealot:** You are an unkillable shock-trooper for the Crimson Dominion, resurrected repeatedly by the priests of the Iron Arbiter to continue fighting.


---

# Chapter 26: Custom Player Options

The Shattered Expanse is a world defined by the sea, ancient magic, and cutthroat factions. Standard backgrounds and class archetypes can be adapted, but to truly root your character in the lore of the Charted Waters, consider the following setting-specific options.

---

## 1. Setting-Specific Backgrounds

### Dominion Legionnaire
You were a soldier, inquisitor, or logistics officer for the Crimson Dominion. You are used to strict hierarchy, ironclad law, and the belief that order must be imposed on a chaotic world. You may still be active duty, or you may be a deserter fleeing the Iron Arbiter's wrath.

- **Skill Proficiencies:** Intimidation, Athletics
- **Tool Proficiencies:** One type of gaming set, land vehicles
- **Equipment:** A legionnaire's uniform, an insignia of rank, a set of bone dice, and a pouch containing 15 gp.
- **Feature: Military Bureaucracy.** You know how to navigate the complex checkpoints and red tape of the Crimson Dominion. You can often secure an audience with lower-to-mid-tier Dominion officers, and you can comfortably requisition basic, non-magical supplies (rations, standard ammunition, simple weapons) from a Dominion garrison, provided you are in good standing.

### Consortium Smuggler
You grew up in the shadows of the Grand Merchant Consortium. While the Guildmasters deal in golden ledgers, you deal in the black market. You know how to move illicit goods, forge manifests, and avoid harbor inspections.

- **Skill Proficiencies:** Deception, Sleight of Hand
- **Tool Proficiencies:** Forgery kit, Navigator's tools
- **Equipment:** A crowbar, dark common clothes with a hidden pocket, a forged shipping manifest, and a pouch containing 15 gp.
- **Feature: Black Market Contacts.** Whenever you arrive in a major port or settlement, you know the subtle signs to look for to locate a fence or a smuggler's den. You can easily find buyers for stolen or illegal goods, and you can purchase illicit items without drawing the attention of local authorities.

### Silk House Exile
You are a survivor of the terrifying Drowned Silk Houses. You might be a rogue Drow who rejected the Deep Mother, or a surface-dweller who was captured and managed to escape the abyssal slave pens. You carry the trauma of the deep dark with you.

- **Skill Proficiencies:** Stealth, Survival
- **Language:** Undercommon
- **Equipment:** A dagger made of black coral, a set of dark, tattered clothes, a trinket from the deep ocean, and a pouch containing 10 gp.
- **Feature: Sunless Survivor.** You have a knack for finding safe harbor in subterranean, underwater, or extremely hostile environments. You can easily identify safe places to rest in natural cave systems or ruins, and you have an instinctive sense of depth and direction when deprived of sunlight.

### Spirit Keeper Warden
You are a defender of the Verdant Expanse. You revere the Slumbering Leviathans and view the industrialization of the empires as a blight upon the world. You have spent your life listening to the song of the ocean.

- **Skill Proficiencies:** Nature, Animal Handling
- **Tool Proficiencies:** Herbalism kit
- **Equipment:** A staff carved from driftwood, a vial of pure aether-water, a set of traveler's clothes, and a pouch containing 10 gp.
- **Feature: Voice of the Reef.** You can interpret the subtle signs of sea-life (the migration of fish, the blooming of kelp, the songs of whales) to accurately predict mundane weather patterns for the next 24 hours, and you can sense when a Slumbering Leviathan is beginning to stir in the region.

### Independent Salvager
You belong to the loose collective of scavengers who dive into the treacherous Unwritten Sea to retrieve artifacts of the First Captains. You are part historian, part mechanic, and part daredevil.

- **Skill Proficiencies:** Investigation, History
- **Tool Proficiencies:** Tinker's tools, Thieves' tools
- **Equipment:** A set of tinker's tools, a strange, broken technological artifact, a set of sturdy diving clothes, and a pouch containing 15 gp.
- **Feature: Junk Tinkerer.** You can scavenge parts from your surroundings to temporarily repair mundane broken items (like a snapped axle, a rusted lock, or a leaky hull) or jury-rig simple, single-use tools (like a makeshift pulley or a temporary patch). These repairs last for 24 hours before breaking again.

---

## 2. New Subclasses

### Bard: College of the Shanty
Bards of the College of the Shanty are not delicate lute-strummers playing in royal courts. They are loud, rhythmic leaders who use booming voices and percussive instruments to keep a crew working in perfect synchronization. Their magic is woven into the beat of the drum and the stomp of the boot, capable of turning a disorganized mob into a unified, terrifying force.

**Bonus Proficiencies**
When you join the College of the Shanty at 3rd level, you gain proficiency with water vehicles and one type of artisan's tools (such as carpenter's tools or cook's utensils). If you are already proficient, you can choose another tool proficiency.

**Heave Away**
At 3rd level, you can use your Bardic Inspiration to coordinate your allies. When an ally rolls a Bardic Inspiration die you gave them, they gain temporary hit points equal to the number rolled + your Charisma modifier. Furthermore, if they use the die to make a melee weapon attack, they can push the target 5 feet away from them on a hit.

**Rhythm of the Sea**
At 6th level, your voice cuts through the chaos of battle. As an action, you can begin a rhythmic chant. For 1 minute, as long as you maintain concentration (as if concentrating on a spell), you and friendly creatures within 30 feet of you who can hear you ignore difficult terrain and have advantage on saving throws against being charmed or frightened.

**Thunderous Chorus**
At 14th level, you can unleash the accumulated energy of your crew's synchronization. When a creature within 30 feet of you deals damage to a target while under the effect of your *Rhythm of the Sea*, you can use your reaction to expend one use of your Bardic Inspiration. Roll the die; the target takes additional thunder damage equal to two rolls of your Bardic Inspiration die, and must succeed on a Strength saving throw against your spell save DC or be knocked prone.

---

### Barbarian: Path of the Leviathan
Barbarians who walk the Path of the Leviathan emulate the terrifying, crushing power of the ancient sea beasts that slumber beneath the waves. They do not just channel anger; they channel the abyssal pressure, the freezing cold, and the unstoppable mass of the deep ocean.

**Abyssal Adaptation**
Starting when you choose this path at 3rd level, you gain a swimming speed equal to your walking speed, and you can breathe underwater. Additionally, you ignore any drawbacks caused by a deep underwater environment.

**Crushing Pressure**
At 3rd level, your rage manifests as a field of heavy, aquatic pressure. While you are raging, the ground within 10 feet of you is difficult terrain for your enemies. If an enemy starts its turn within this area, its speed is reduced by 10 feet until the start of its next turn.

**Leviathan's Mass**
At 6th level, you can channel the unstoppable momentum of a sea beast. While raging, you have advantage on Strength (Athletics) checks made to grapple or shove a creature. Additionally, you count as one size larger when determining your carrying capacity and the weight you can push, drag, or lift.

**Depths of Despair**
At 10th level, you can exude the terrifying, cold isolation of the sunless sea. As an action, you can force all creatures of your choice within 30 feet of you to make a Wisdom saving throw (DC equal to 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Constitution modifier). On a failed save, a creature is frightened of you for 1 minute. A frightened creature can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. Once you use this feature, you cannot do so again until you finish a long rest.

**Tsunami Strike**
At 14th level, when you hit a creature with a melee weapon attack while raging, you can unleash a shockwave of force. The target and all enemies within 15 feet of it behind it must make a Strength saving throw (DC equal to 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Strength modifier). On a failed save, they take 4d6 bludgeoning damage and are knocked prone. On a successful save, they take half damage and are not knocked prone. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Constitution modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a long rest.

---

## 3. Setting-Specific Feats

If your DM allows the use of feats, you may choose the following options, which represent unique adaptations to the Shattered Expanse.

### Sea Legs
You have spent so much time on the unsteady decks of ships that the rolling waves feel more natural than solid ground.
- Increase your Dexterity or Constitution score by 1, to a maximum of 20.
- You have advantage on saving throws and ability checks against being knocked prone.
- Climbing rigging or swimming does not cost you extra movement.

### Aether-Scarred
You survived prolonged exposure to the chaotic, magical radiation of the Unwritten Sea, leaving you physically marked and magically altered.
- Increase your Constitution or Charisma score by 1, to a maximum of 20.
- You gain resistance to either Force or Lightning damage (your choice when you take this feat).
- You learn the *thaumaturgy* cantrip. When you cast it, the effects often have a slightly mutated, unsettling, or oceanic flair (e.g., your voice sounds like grinding tectonic plates, or the flames you flicker turn bioluminescent blue). You can cast this cantrip using Constitution as your spellcasting ability.


---
